Generalised Springer correspondence for 𝐙 / m \mathbf{Z}/m -graded Lie algebras
Wille Liu

Abstract
Résumé Let G G be a simple simply connected complex algebraic group and let 𝔤 ∗ \mathfrak{g}_{*} be a 𝐙 / m \mathbf{Z}/m -grading on its Lie algebra 𝔤 \mathfrak{g} . In a recent series of articles, G. Lusztig and Z. Yun, studied the classification of simple G 0 G_{0} -equivariant perverse sheaves on the nilpotent cone of 𝔤 i \mathfrak{g}_{i} for i ∈ 𝐙 / m i\in\mathbf{Z}/m , where G 0 G_{0} is the exponentiation of the degree zero piece 𝔤 0 \mathfrak{g}_{0} . They proved a decomposition of the equivariant derived category of ℓ \ell -adic sheaves on the nilpotent cone of 𝔤 i \mathfrak{g}_{i} into blocks, each generated by a certain cuspidal local system via spiral inductions . We prove a conjecture of them, which predicts the bijectivity of a map from 1) the set of simple perverse sheaves in a fixed block to 2) the set of simple modules of a block of a (trigonometric) degenerate double affine Hecke algebra (dDAHA). This is a dDAHA analogue of the Deligne–Langlands correspondence for affine Hecke algebras proven by Kazhdan–Lusztig. Our results generalise a previous work of E. Vasserot, where the perverse sheaves in the principal block were considered.



\alttitle

Correspondance de Springer généralisée pour les algèbres de Lie
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graduées

{altabstract}

Soient
G
un groupe algébrique complexe simple simplement connexe et
\mathfrak{g}_{*}
une
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graduation sur
\mathfrak{g}=\Lie G
. Dans une série récente d’articles, G. Lusztig et Z. Yun ont étudié la classification des faisceaux pervers simples
G_{0}
-équivariants sur le cône nilpotent de
\mathfrak{g}_{i}
pour
i\in\mathbf{Z}/m
, où
G_{0}
est l’exponentialisé de la composante de degré nul
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
. Ils ont établi une décomposition en blocs de la catégorie dérivée équivariante des faisceaux
\ell
-adiques sur le cône nilpotent de
\mathfrak{g}_{i}
; chacun des blocs est engendré par un certain système local cuspidal via les
inductions spirales
. Nous démontrons leur conjecture qui prédit la bijectivité d’une application de 1) l’ensemble des faisceaux pervers simples dans un bloc donné dans 2) l’ensemble des modules simples d’une algèbre de Hecke doublement affine dégénérée. Ceci est pour les algèbres de Hecke doublement affines dégénérées un résultat analogue à la correspondance de Deligne–Langlands, démontrée par Kazhdan–Lusztig et portant sur les algèbres de Hecke affines. Nos résultats généralisent ceux d’un travail d’E. Vasserot, dans lequel seuls les faisceaux pervers dans le bloc principal étaient pris en compte.

Introduction

In the present article, we establish a generalised Springer correspondence for
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graded Lie algebras and (trigonometric) degenerate double affine Hecke algebras, which was conjectured by Lusztig–Yun
[
19
]
. The main result is
subsection 9.11
, which confirms the multiplicity-one conjecture proposed in
[
19
]
and can be viewed as a generalised Springer correspondence in the sense of Lusztig
[
15
,
13
]
, for certain degenerate double affine Hecke algebras (dDAHAs) with possibly unequal parameters.

Generalised Springer correspondence

G. Lusztig in
[
15
]
generalised the classical Springer correspondence on reductive groups by introducing cuspidal local systems.

Let
G
be a complex connected reductive group and let
\mathfrak{g}=\Lie G
denote its Lie algebra, on which
G
acts by the adjoint action. A
cuspidal pair

(\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
on
\mathfrak{g}
consists of a nilpotent
G
-orbit
\mathrm{O}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}
together with an irreducible
G
-equivariant local system
\mathscr{C}
on
\mathrm{O}
such that the following condition holds : for every strict parabolic subalgebra
\mathfrak{p}\subsetneq\mathfrak{g}
with unipotent radical
\mathfrak{u}\subseteq\mathfrak{p}
and for any element
x\in\mathrm{O}
, we have
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{c}((x+\mathfrak{u})\cap\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})=0
.

Given the reductive group
G
, the following statements are proven in
[
15
]
:

(i)

There is a partition of the set
\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}})
of isomorphism classes of simple
G
-equivariant perverse sheaves on the nilpotent cone
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}
into
series
:

\displaystyle\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}})=\bigsqcup_{\xi}\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}})_{\xi},

where
\xi
runs over all triples
(M,\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
up to conjugation, where
M\subset G
is a Levi subgroup and
(\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
is a cuspidal pair on the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{m}
of
M
.

(ii)

For each triple
\xi=\left(M,\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C}\right)
, there is a crystallographic finite Coxeter group
W_{\xi}
, called
relative Weyl group
, with a bijection

\displaystyle\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}})_{\xi}\xlongrightarrow{\sim}\Irr(\mathbf{C}W_{\xi}\mof).

The series
\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}})_{\xi}
is defined using the
Lusztig–Spaltenstein parabolic induction
. Given such a triple
\xi=(M,\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
, let
P
be a parabolic subgroup of
G
containing
M
as Levi factor and let
U
be the unipotent radical of
P
with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{u}=\Lie U
. Consider the following diagram of stacks :

[G\backslash\mathfrak{g}]\xleftarrow{a}[G\backslash(G\times^{P}\left(\mathrm{O}\oplus\mathfrak{u}\right))]\xrightarrow{\cong}[P\backslash(\mathrm{O}\oplus\mathfrak{u})]\xrightarrow{b}[M\backslash\mathfrak{m}],

where
\mathrm{O}\oplus\mathfrak{u}
is the image of the addition map
\mathrm{O}\times\mathfrak{u}\xrightarrow{+}\mathfrak{p}
.
The complex
\mathbf{I}^{\xi}=a_{*}b^{*}\mathscr{C}[\dim G\times^{P}(\mathrm{O}\oplus\mathfrak{u})]
is a
G
-equivariant semisimple perverse sheaf and
\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}\right)_{\xi}
consists of the simple constituents of
\mathbf{I}^{\xi}
. The bijection in statement (ii) was constructed by means of a ring isomorphism
\mathbf{C}W_{\xi}\xrightarrow{\cong}\End(\mathbf{I}^{\xi})
.

Equivariant enhancements

Let
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}})
be the equivariant category of Bernstein–Lunts
[
1
]
. In the above statements (i) and (ii), the
G
-equivariance is only a condition on perverse sheaves and higher extensions between perverse sheaves in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}})
play no rôle there.

In
[
22
]
, higher extensions are taken into account for the statement (i). It is enhanced into the following :

(i
+
)

There is an orthogonal decomposition of triangulated category :

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}})=\bigoplus_{\xi}\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}})_{\xi},

where
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{G}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}})_{\xi}
is the thick triangulated subcategory generated by
\mathbf{I}^{\xi}
.

For each triple
\xi
as above, there is a
graded affine Hecke algebra
(graded AHA)
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}
: it is a degenerate version of the affine Hecke algebras introduced by G. Lusztig
[
9
]
, see
subsection 1.1
for the definition of the graded AHA in our setting. Let
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}=\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be a one-dimensional torus which acts linearly on the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
by weight
-2
and trivially on the group
G
. The perverse sheaf
\mathbf{I}^{\xi}
acquires a
G\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
-equivariant structure. In
[
3
]
and
[
14
]
, statement (ii) above is enhanced into the following :

(ii
+
)

There is an isomorphism of graded rings

(0.1)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\xrightarrow{\cong}\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbf{Z}}\Hom_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{G\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}})}(\mathbf{I}^{\xi},\mathbf{I}^{\xi}[k]).

The summands of the right-hand side vanish except for
k\in 2\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Taking quotient by the graded radicals of both sides of (
), we recover the aforementioned isomorphism
\mathbf{C}W_{\xi}\cong\End(\mathbf{I}^{\xi})
.
One can also replace
G
with
G\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
in statement (i
+
).

Perverse sheaves on
\mathbf{Z}
-graded Lie algebras

Keep the reductive group
G
as above. If
\lambda\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(G)
is a cocharacter, then it gives rise to a
\mathbf{Z}
-grading on
\mathfrak{g}
by

\mathfrak{g}=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\mathfrak{g}_{n},\quad\mathfrak{g}_{n}=\left\{x\in\mathfrak{g}\;;\;\lambda(t)x=t^{n}x,\quad\forall t\in\mathbf{C}^{\times}\right\}.

Fix
\eta\in\mathbf{Z}_{\neq 0}
. The fixed-point subgroup
G^{\lambda}
acts on the subspace
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}
by the adjoint action. The number of
G^{\lambda}
-orbits in
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}
is finite. The classification of
G^{\lambda}
-equivariant simple perverse sheaves on
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}
proven in
[
13
,
14
,
16
,
3
]
is the following :

(i’)

There is a partition of the set
\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G^{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\eta})
of isomorphism classes of simple
G^{\lambda}
-equivariant perverse sheaves on the subspace
\mathfrak{g}^{\lambda}_{\eta}
into
series
:

\displaystyle\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G^{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\eta})=\bigsqcup_{\xi}\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G^{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\eta})_{\xi},

where
\xi
runs over all triples
(M,\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
up to
G^{\lambda}
-conjugation, where
M\subset G
is a
\lambda
-stable Levi subgroup and
(\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
is a cuspidal pair on
\mathfrak{m}
such that
\mathrm{O}\cap\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}\neq\emptyset
.

(ii’)

For each such triple
\xi=\left(M,\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C}\right)
, there is a bijection

\displaystyle\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G}(\mathfrak{g}_{\eta})_{\xi}\xlongrightarrow{\sim}\Irr\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\mof_{(\lambda,\eta/2)},

where
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\mof_{(\lambda,\eta/2)}
is a
block
of the category of finite dimensional
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}
-modules, which depends on
\lambda
and
\eta/2
.

The series
\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G^{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\eta})_{\xi}
is defined using a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded version of parabolic induction of the cuspidal local system
\mathscr{C}
. Statement (ii’) can be derived from the isomorphism (
) by the technique of equivariant localisation. We will review these results in
section 1
below.

One might want to generalise the above story to loop groups / affine Kac–Moody groups. On the algebraic side, the affinisation of graded affine Hecke algebra is the degenerate double affine Hecke algebra (dDAHA). On the geometric side, the affinisation of the Springer resolution is the affine Springer resolution. However, instead of working on the infinite-dimensional geometry of the loop groups and affine Springer resolutions, we will consider cyclic gradings on simple Lie algebras, see
subsection 2.1
for a heuristic explanation of the relation between
\mathbf{Z}
-gradings on loop Lie algebras and cyclic gradings on simple Lie algebras.

Perverse sheaves on
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graded Lie algebras and dDAHA

Suppose that we are given a simply connected simple complex algebraic group
G
together with a group homomorphism
\theta:\mu_{m}\to\Aut(G)
, where
m\in\mathbf{Z}_{>0}
and
\mu_{m}=\left\{z\in\mathbf{C}^{\times}\;;\;z^{m}=1\right\}
. The map
\theta
gives rise to a
\mathbf{Z}/m
-grading on the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}=\Lie G
by

\mathfrak{g}=\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{k}}\in\mathbf{Z}/m}\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{k}}},\quad\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{k}}}=\left\{x\in\mathfrak{g}\;;\;\theta(\zeta)x=\zeta^{k}x,\quad\forall\zeta\in\mu_{m}\right\}.

Also let
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}=G^{\theta}
denote the fixed points of
\theta
in
G
. Fix
\eta\in\mathbf{Z}_{\neq 0}
and let
\underline{\smash{\eta}}\in\mathbf{Z}/m
be its congruence class. In
[
18
]
, G. Lusztig and Z. Yun addressed the problem of partitioning simple
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}
-equivariant perverse sheaves on the nilpotent cone
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}=\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}\cap\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
into
series
by introducing the crucial geometric tool of
spiral induction
. The result can be stated as follows :

(i”)

There is a partition of the set
\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}})
of isomorphism classes of simple
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}
-equivariant perverse sheaves on
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
into
series
:

\displaystyle\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}})=\bigsqcup_{\xi}\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}})_{\xi},

where
\xi
runs over all
admissible systems

(M,\mathfrak{m}_{*},\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
, introduced in
[
18
, §3]
, see
subsection 3.4
. Moreover, the orthogonal decomposition also holds :

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}})=\bigoplus_{\xi}\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}})_{\xi}.

In
[
19
]
, the authors defined for each admissible system
\xi
an affine root system and a dDAHA
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
, see
subsection 5.1
. The main result of
[
19
]
is the construction of an action of
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
on an infinite sum of the spiral induction from various spirals

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{\xi}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}_{*}}\Ind_{\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}}^{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}\mathscr{C}.

They constructed an action of
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
on the perverse cohomology
\prescript{p}{}{\mathscr{H}}\mathbf{I}^{\xi}=\bigoplus_{k}\prescript{p}{}{\mathscr{H}}^{k}\mathbf{I}^{\xi}
and they conjectured that the multiplicity space of each simple constituent of the perverse cohomology
\prescript{p}{}{\mathscr{H}}\mathbf{I}^{\xi}
is a simple
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
-module and this yields an injection

\displaystyle\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}})_{\xi}\hookrightarrow\Irr\mathcal{O}(\mathbf{H}_{\xi}),

where
\mathcal{O}(\mathbf{H}_{\xi})
is the category of integrable
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
-modules, see
subsection 9.4
. The main result of this article confirms this conjecture :

(ii”)

Theorem

(=
subsection 9.11
)
.

There is a bijection

\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}})_{\xi}\xlongrightarrow{\sim}\Irr\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{x},\eta/2m}(\mathbf{H}_{\xi}),

where
\Irr\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{x},\eta/2m}(\mathbf{H}_{\xi})
is a block of
\mathcal{O}(\mathbf{H}_{\xi})
.

Remark
.

It turns out that, in statement (i”), a naive
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graded version of parabolic induction is not enough to generate the series
\Irr\mathrm{Perv}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}})_{\xi}
. This is related to the Gelfand–Kirillov dimension of simple modules of the dDAHA
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
: only those simple perverse sheaves whose corresponding simple
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
-modules are of maximal GK-dimension appear in the parabolic induction. This phenomenon is explained in
[
8
]
. The spiral induction of the cuspidal local system
\mathscr{C}
introduced in
[
18
]
is the right substitute. The notion of a spiral is closely related to parahoric subalgebras of loop Lie groups. We refer to
subsection 3.3
for its definition.

We achieve the theorem by constructing in
subsection 5.8
a ring homomorphism

\Phi:\mathbf{H}_{\xi}\to\Ext^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}}\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}}(\mathbf{I}^{\xi},\mathbf{I}^{\xi})_{0}=:\widehat{\mathcal{H}},

where
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}}
is the semi-direct product of
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}
with a
loop rotation torus
(
section 2
) acting on it. The ring
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
is the completion of the extension algebra of
\mathbf{I}^{\xi}
at the augmentation ideal
\mathrm{H}^{>0}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}}\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}}
of equivariant coefficient ring (
section 5
for the precise statements). We then show in
section 8
that when
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
is equipped with a suitable topology, the image of
\Phi
is dense. The technique of convolution algebras of
[
2
]
can be applied and yields a correspondence (
subsection 9.5
) between simple smooth
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
-modules and simple perverse sheaves. Finally, making use of the density of the image of
\Phi
, we construct in
subsection 9.9
an equivalence between the category of smooth
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
-modules and
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{x},\eta/2m}(\mathbf{H}_{\xi})
, which provides an bijection of simple objects between both categories. These two bijections provide a geometric parametrisation of simple modules in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{x},\eta/2m}(\mathbf{H}_{\xi})
. Our approach to construct
\Phi
is via inducing the map (
) from all parabolic subalgebras of
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
, which are graded affine Hecke algebras. The proof of the density of
\Phi
involves analysing the geometry of the Steinberg varieties. We refer to
section 2
for an explanation of the strategy.

Related works

The case where
M
is a maximal torus and
\mathscr{C}
is trivial has previously been studied by Vasserot in
[
25
]
using equivariant K-homology over Kashiwara’s flag manifold. The double affine Hecke algebras (DAHA) obtained are those with equal parameters.

In
[
24
]
, M. Varagnolo and E. Vasserot used the action of DAHA constructed in
[
25
]
to classify the
positive slopes
for which the spherical simple module of the dDAHA (with equal parameters) is finite dimensional. The Springer fibres involved are those which are over the regular semisimple locus of the affine Lie algebra. Our classification result
subsection 9.11
works essentially for
negative slopes
, where we consider torus fixed-points of Springer fibres over nilpotent elements of the affine Lie algebra. Nevertheless, with our construction of the map
\Phi
, which works equally well for positive slopes, one can hope for a generalisation of the result of
[
24
]
to dDAHAs at unequal parameters. We hope to study the relation between these two opposite cases in a future work.

In
[
26
]
, Z. Yun constructed an action of the dDAHA (with equal parameters) using parabolic Hitchin moduli spaces of principal bundles, which is a global version of the affine Springer resolution. This result is later used in
[
20
]
to further geometrise the results of
[
24
]
. One can hope for a generalisation of these results with cuspidal local systems and dDAHAs with unequal parameters.

Organisation of the article

The sections
section 1
–
section 4
are mostly a recollection of previously known results. We reformulate them in a slightly different language.

In
section 1
, we review the sheaf-theoretic construction of graded AHAs given in
[
13
]
,
[
3
]
and
[
14
]
. We prove in
subsection 1.17
that this construction is compatible with parabolic induction.

In
section 2
, we explain the relation between the loop Lie algebras and
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graded Lie algebra as well as the relation between affine Springer resolution and the spirals. We sketch the construction of
\Phi
and the strategy of the proof of the density of
\Phi
.

In
section 3
, we review the geometric setup of
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graded Lie algebras introduced by Lusztig–Yun. We recall the part of their results that is important for the purpose of this article. In particular, we recall the notion of spirals and spiral induction.

In
section 4
, we review the Coxeter complex, relative root system and relative affine Weyl group introduced in
[
19
]
. We discuss their relation with spirals.

In
section 5
, we define the dDAHA
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
and the completed extension algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
. We construct also the homomorphism
\Phi:\mathbf{H}_{\xi}\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
. This is done by inducing the map
\Phi
from parabolic subalgebras of
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
by means of spiral induction.

The sections
section 6
and
section 7
are preparation for the proof of the density theorem
section 8
.

In
section 6
, we describe our principal geometric objects : varieties
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
,
\mathcal{T}^{\nu}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
, which play the role of fixed point components of the product of partial flag variety, the partial affine Springer resolution and the Steinberg variety, respectively. The filtration by Bruhat order of the varieties
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
will be important in the analysis of the convolution algebra. We prove in
subsection 6.17
that the convolution product on
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
respects the Bruhat order and we describe the good Bruhat strata.

In
section 7
, we interpret the algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
as a convolution algebra. The main technical results are
subsection 7.10
and
subsection 7.17
, which will play crucial rôles in the proof of
section 8

In
section 8
, making use of the calculations of
section 7
, we show that the image of
\Phi
is dense.

In
section 9
, we derive some consequences of the density of
\Phi
. In particular, we establish the multiplicity-one conjecture of
[
19
]
. Simple integrable
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
-modules with prescribed eigenvalues are classified in
subsection 9.11
. We also relate simple modules with the cohomology of torus fixed points of affine Springer fibres in
subsection 9.14
.

In
section 10
, we work out the correspondence stated in
subsection 9.11
in a special case.

In
section 11
, we explain the modifications needed for
twisted
affine root systems.

Convention and notation

All schemes and algebraic stacks concerned will be over the field of complex number
\mathbf{C}
, which can be replaced by any algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
or of sufficiently large characteristic (depending on the type of
G
).

For any algebraic stack
\mathcal{X}
, we denote by
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\left(\mathcal{X}\right)
the bounded derived category of constructible
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}
-sheaves on
\mathcal{X}
defined by Laszlo–Olsson
[
7
]
. We denote
\mathbf{k}=\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}
.

For any algebraic group
G
acting on
X
(on the left), we denote by
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{G}\left(X\right)=\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\left(\left[G\backslash X\right]\right)
the
G
-equivariant bounded derived category of
\mathbf{k}
-sheaves on
X
, or equivalently bounded derived category of
\mathbf{k}
-sheaves on the quotient stack
\left[G\backslash X\right]
. We denote
\mathrm{Perv}_{G}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{G}\left(X\right)
the subcategory of complexes whose image in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(X)
is perverse  ; those are perverse sheaves on the stack
[G\backslash X]
up to a shift. Denote by
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G}=\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathbf{k})
the coefficient ring of the
G
-equivariant cohomology. For objects
\mathscr{F},\mathscr{G}\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{G}(X)
, we put
\Ext^{n}_{G}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{G})=\Hom_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{G}(X)}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{G}[n])
and
\Ext^{*}_{G}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{G})=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\Ext^{n}_{G}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{G})
.

On these derived categories, the six operations
\otimes,\scrHom,f_{*},f^{*},f_{!},f^{!}
will be understood as derived functors. We suppress the symbols
\mathrm{R}
and
\mathrm{L}
from
\mathrm{R}f_{*}
,
\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}
, etc. The bi-duality functor will be denoted
\mathbf{D}
, the perverse cohomology functors will be denoted
\prescript{p}{}{\mathscr{H}}^{k}
for
k\in\mathbf{Z}
and for any local system
\mathscr{L}
supported on some locally closed subset, its intersection complex will be denoted
\IC(\mathscr{L})\in\mathrm{Perv}_{G}(X)
.

When a capital Latin letter (
A,B,C,\ldots
) denote an algebraic group, we usually use the corresponding Fraktur small letter (
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c},\ldots
) to denote its Lie algebra. When
G
is an algebraic group, we denote by
Z(G)
or
Z_{G}
its centre and by
\mathfrak{z}_{G}
or
\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{g})
the centre of its Lie algebra.

For any algebraic group
G
, the set of one-parameter subgroups (= cocharacters) is denoted by
\mathbf{X}_{*}(G)
. We will adopt the notion of fractional cocharacters

\mathbf{X}_{*}(G)_{\mathbf{Q}}=\left\{\mu/r\;;\;\mu\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(G),\quad r\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 1}\right\}/\left((\mu,r)\sim(\mu^{\prime},r^{\prime})\Leftrightarrow r^{\prime}\mu=r\mu^{\prime}\right).

We will also consider weight spaces of elements of
\mathbf{X}_{*}(G)_{\mathbf{Q}}
. If
\rho:G\to\Aut(V)
is a rational representation and if
\mu\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(G)_{\mathbf{Q}}
, then for each
r\in\mathbf{Q}
the weight space of
\mu
of weight
r
in
V
is denoted by
\prescript{\mu}{r}{V}
. Namely, let
k\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that
k\mu\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(G)
and
kr\in\mathbf{Z}
, then

\displaystyle\prescript{\mu}{r}{V}=\left\{v\in V\;;\;\Ad_{(k\mu)(t)}(v)=t^{kr}v,\forall t\in\mathbf{C}^{\times}\right\}.

Similarly, if
G
acts on a space
X
, we let
X^{\mu}
denote the fixed point of the cocharacter
k\mu\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(G)
.

For any pair of adjoint functors
(L,R)
, we denote by
\id\to RL
the adjunction unit and by
LR\to\id
the adjunction co-unit.

1
\mathbf{Z}
-graded Lie algebras and graded AHAs revisited

We recall the works of Lusztig
[
15
]

[
14
]

[
16
]
and Evens–Mirković
[
3
]
on cuspidal local systems and graded affine Hecke algebras. At the end of this section, we prove in
subsection 1.17
that their constructions are compatible with the parabolic induction.

1.1
Cuspidal local systems and AHAs

Let
G
be a connected reductive algebraic group over
\mathbf{C}
and
M\subset G
a Levi subgroup. Assume that there is a nilpotent
M
-orbit
\mathrm{O}\subset\mathfrak{m}^{\mathrm{nil}}
and a cuspidal
M
-equivariant local system
\mathscr{C}
on
\mathrm{O}
in the sense of
[
15
]
. The centre
Z_{M}
of
M
may be disconnected but the neutral component
Z_{M}^{\circ}
is a torus. To such a datum
\xi=(M,\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
, Lusztig associated in
[
13
, 2.4]
a
relative root system

(\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}},R_{\xi})
on the space
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}=\mathbf{X}_{*}(Z_{M})\otimes\mathbf{Q}
equipped with the inner product induced from the Killing form of
G
. Let
R^{\prime}_{\xi}\subset\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}^{*}
be the set of non-zero weights of
Z_{M}^{\circ}
which appears in
\mathfrak{g}
. The pair
(\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}},R_{\xi}^{\prime})
is a possibly non-reduced root system. The set of roots
R_{\xi}
is the subset of
R^{\prime}_{\xi}
consisting of indivisible roots.

The
relative Weyl group
is defined to be
W_{\xi}=N_{G}(Z_{M}^{\circ})/M
. It coincides with the Weyl group of
(\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}},R_{\xi})
. It is shown in
[
15
, 9.2]
that the adjoint action of
N_{G}(Z_{M}^{\circ})
on
\mathfrak{m}
preserves the nilpotent orbit
\mathrm{O}
and the cuspidal local system
\mathscr{C}
has a
N_{G}(Z_{M}^{\circ})
-equivariant structure. When
M
is a maximal torus of
G
, the relative root system
(\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}},R_{\xi})
is reduced to the usual root system of
G
.

Let
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}=\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be the multiplicative group which acts on
\mathfrak{g}
by weight -2 and trivially on
G
. For any group
H
, we put
H_{q}=H\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
unless it is defined otherwise. The
M
-equivariant local system
\mathscr{C}
admits a unique
M_{q}
-equivariant enhancement. Choose any parabolic subalgebra
P\subset G
containing
M
as Levi factor and denote by
\pi:\mathfrak{p}\to\mathfrak{m}
the projection. Let

\dot{\mathfrak{g}}=G\times^{P}\pi^{-1}(\mathrm{O})\xrightarrow{a}\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}},\quad\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}=\dot{\mathfrak{g}}\times_{\mathfrak{g}}\dot{\mathfrak{g}}\xhookrightarrow{(p_{1},p_{2})}\dot{\mathfrak{g}}\times\dot{\mathfrak{g}}

be the partial Springer resolution and the Steinberg variety. One extends
\mathscr{C}
to a
G\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
-equivariant local system on
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}
. The cleanness of cuspidal local systems
[
17
]
implies that
a_{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}}\cong a_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}
. By the Verdier duality, there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\Ext^{*}_{G_{q}}(a_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},a_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}})\cong\Ext^{*}_{G_{q}}(p_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},p_{1}^{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}}).

Using the cleanness of
\mathscr{C}
, in
[
3
]
and
[
14
]
the authors defined a convolution product on
\Ext^{*}_{G_{q}}(p_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},p_{1}^{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}})
which agrees with the Yoneda product of the left-hand side of the above isomorphism and they constructed a ring isomorphism

(1.2)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\xrightarrow{\cong}\Ext^{*}_{G_{q}}(p_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},p_{1}^{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}}).

Here
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}
is the
graded affine Hecke algebra
attached to the datum
\xi=(M,\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
introduced in
[
13
]
.

We recall here the definition of
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}
. We pick as above a parabolic subgroup
P\subset G
which contains
M
as Levi factor. The parabolic
P
yields a set of positive roots
R_{\xi}^{+,P}=\left\{\alpha\in R_{\xi}\;;\;\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset\right\}
and a base
\Delta^{P}_{\xi}\subset R_{\xi}^{+,P}
. The relative Weyl group
W_{\xi}
becomes a Coxeter group with the set of generators
\langle s_{\alpha}\;;\;\alpha\in\Delta^{P}_{\xi}\rangle
. The graded affine Hecke algebra
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}^{P}
associated with the datum
(\xi,\Delta^{P}_{\xi})
is the associative algebra over the polynomial ring
\mathbf{k}[u]
generated by the sets
\left\{x^{\mu}\right\}_{\mu\in\mathbf{X}^{*}(Z^{\circ}_{M})}
and
\left\{s_{\alpha}\right\}_{\alpha\in\Delta^{P}_{\xi}}
subject to the following relations :

\displaystyle x^{\mu}+x^{\nu}=x^{\mu+\nu},\quad\mathbf{k}[s_{\alpha}\;;\;\alpha\in\Delta^{P}_{\xi}]\cong\mathbf{k}W_{\xi}

\displaystyle s_{\alpha}x^{\mu}-x^{s_{\alpha}(\mu)}s_{\alpha}=uc_{\alpha}\langle\mu,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle.

where
c_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 2}
is a constant defined in
[
13
, 2.10]
, which depends on
\xi
(see also
subsection 5.1
). In particular, the set
\{x^{\mu}\}_{\mu\in\mathbf{X}^{*}(Z^{\circ}_{M})}
generates in
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}
a polynomial subalgebra
\mathbf{k}[\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{k}}]=\Sym\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{k}}^{*}
, where
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{k}}=\mathbf{X}_{*}(Z_{M}^{\circ})\otimes\mathbf{k}
,
\mathfrak{z}^{*}_{M,\mathbf{k}}=\mathbf{X}^{*}(Z_{M}^{\circ})\otimes\mathbf{k}
. If
P
and
P^{\prime}
are two parabolic subgroups of
G
which have
M
as Levi factor, then there is an element
\dot{w}\in N_{G}(Z^{\circ}_{M})
such that
\dot{w}P\dot{w}^{-1}=P^{\prime}
. The image of
\dot{w}
in the quotient
W_{\xi}
, denoted by
w
, then yields an isomorphism of based root system
(\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}},\Delta^{P}_{\xi})\cong(\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}},\Delta^{P^{\prime}}_{\xi})
and hence a (canonical) isomorphism of algebras
\underline{\mathbf{H}}^{P}_{\xi}\cong\underline{\mathbf{H}}^{P^{\prime}}_{\xi}
. The graded affine Hecke algebra attached to
\xi
is the inverse limit
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}=\varprojlim_{P}\underline{\mathbf{H}}^{P}_{\xi}
, so that for each choice of parabolic
P
, there is an canonical isomorphism
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\cong\underline{\mathbf{H}}^{P}_{\xi}
.

According to the eigenvalues of the action of the central subalgebra
\mathbf{k}[\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{k}}]^{W_{\xi}}\otimes\mathbf{k}[u]\subset\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}
, the category of finite-dimensional
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}
-modules can be decomposed into blocks :

\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\mof_{\mathrm{fd}}=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbf{k}}\;\bigoplus_{x\in\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{k}}/W_{\xi}}\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\mof_{(x,r)}.

1.3
Simple modules of the graded AHA

Fix
\xi
as in
subsection 1.1
. We shall explain the Deligne–Langlands–Lusztig parametrisation of simple
\underline{\smash{\mathbf{H}}}_{\xi}
-modules.

Let
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(G)
and
\eta\in\mathbf{Z}
. Put
{\lambda_{q}}=(\lambda_{0},\eta/2)\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(M_{q})_{\mathbf{Q}}
. Assume that
\mathfrak{m}^{\lambda_{q}}\cap\mathrm{O}\neq\emptyset
. We have the following commutative square :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{{\lambda_{q}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathfrak{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}^{{\lambda_{q}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\rho}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

\dot{\mathfrak{g}}=G\times^{P}\pi^{-1}(\mathrm{O})\xrightarrow{a}\mathfrak{g}
: partial Springer resolution

\mathfrak{g}^{{\lambda_{q}}},\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{{\lambda_{q}}}
:
{\lambda_{q}}
-fixed points

\rho,\dot{\rho}
: inclusions of
{\lambda_{q}}
-fixed points

The following equivariant localisation formula is proven in
[
3
, 4.10]
:

\displaystyle\Ext^{*}_{G_{q}}(a_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},a_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}})_{{\lambda_{q}}}\cong\Ext^{*}_{G^{\lambda_{0}}_{q}}\left(a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}\right)_{0},

where the subscript
{\lambda_{q}}
means the completion of the extension algebra at
{\lambda_{q}}
, equipped with the adic topology and the subscript
0
means the completion at the augmentation ideal
\mathfrak{o}=\mathrm{H}^{>0}_{G^{\lambda_{0}}_{q}}
. We will explain in
subsection 1.16
about the completion. Combining this with (
), we obtain an injective ring map with dense image

(1.4)

\displaystyle\Phi:\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\to\Ext^{*}_{G^{\lambda_{0}}_{q}}\left(a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}\right)_{0}=:\widehat{\mathcal{H}}.

Remark 1.5
.

(i)

In fact, the formula (
) was stated and proven in terms of “quotient by
\mathfrak{o}
” rather than “completion at
\mathfrak{o}
”. Nevertheless, its proof can be easily adapted for completion.

(ii)

Instead of completion at
{\lambda_{q}}
, one can take completion at
(\lambda_{0}/m,\eta/2m)
for any
m\in\mathbf{k}^{\times}
and the resulting completions will be isomorphic. In particular, we may take the completion at any fractional cocharacters.

Choose an
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple
\phi=(e,h,f)
in
\mathfrak{m}
with
e\in\mathfrak{m}^{\lambda_{q}}\cap\mathrm{O}
,
h\in\prescript{\lambda_{0}}{0}{\mathfrak{m}}
and
f\in\prescript{\lambda_{0}}{-\eta}{\mathfrak{m}}
and set

(1.6)

\displaystyle Z^{q}_{M}(\phi)=\left\{(g,q)\in M\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}\;;\;\Ad_{g}(e)=q^{2}e,\;\Ad_{g}(h)=h,\;\Ad_{g}(f)=q^{-2}f\right\}.

The group
Z^{q}_{M}(\phi)
is commutative and there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\iota:Z_{M}\times\mathbf{C}^{\times}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}Z^{q}_{M}(\phi)

\displaystyle(g,q)

\displaystyle\mapsto(gq^{h},q).

*
*

*

This isomorphism is technical but extremely important in the calculation of the equivariant cohomology with cuspidal local systems. We will invoke it several times.

The image of the cocharacter
{\lambda_{q}}\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(M_{q})_{\mathbf{Q}}
lies in
Z^{q}_{M}(\phi)
, so we can put
(\lambda^{M},\eta/2)=\iota^{*}\lambda_{q}
with
\lambda^{M}\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(Z_{M})_{\mathbf{Q}}
. The element
\lambda^{M}
does not depend on the choice of
\phi
.

By the general theory of extension algebras (see
[
2
, Ch 8]
), the simple
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
-modules are indexed by the simple constituents of the perverse cohomology
\prescript{p}{}{\mathscr{H}}a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}
. On the other hand, the map
\Phi
induces an equivalence between
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\mof_{(\lambda^{M},\eta/2)}
and the category of finite-dimensional
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
-modules. These considerations together yield the following geometric parametrisation :

Theorem 1.7

(
[
14
, 8.14]
)
.

The ring map
\Phi
induces a natural bijection

\displaystyle\Irr(\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\mof_{(\lambda^{M},\eta/2)})\quad\xlongrightarrow{\sim}\quad\left\{\text{simple perverse sheaf $\subseteq\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbf{Z}}\prescript{p}{}{\mathscr{H}}^{k}a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}$}\right\}/\sim.

1.8
\mathbf{Z}
-graded Lie algebras

In
[
16
]
, Lusztig introduced a geometric framework for studying the fixed-point variety
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\lambda_{q}}
and the complex
a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}
that appears in the previous theorem. We briefly recall the basic constructions.

Let
G
be a complex connected connected reductive group as above and let
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(G)
be a cocharacter. Then
\lambda_{0}
gives rise to a
\mathbf{Z}
-grading on the Lie algebra :

\mathfrak{g}=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\mathfrak{g}_{n},\quad\mathfrak{g}_{n}=\prescript{\lambda_{0}}{n}{\mathfrak{g}}=\left\{x\in\mathfrak{g}\;;\;\Ad_{\lambda_{0}(t)}x=t^{n}x,\quad\forall t\in\mathbf{C}^{\times}\right\}.

Let
G_{0}=G^{\lambda_{0}}
denote the subgroup of
\lambda_{0}
-fixed points of
G
. For any subset
\Sigma\subset G
, we will write
\Sigma_{0}=\Sigma\cap G_{0}
and for any subset
\Sigma\subset\mathfrak{g}
, we write
U_{n}=U\cap\mathfrak{g}_{n}
.

Let
\eta\in\mathbf{Z}_{\neq 0}
. We have
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}
and the adjoint action of
G_{0}
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}
has a finite number of orbits. Let
P\subset G
be a
\lambda_{0}
-stable parabolic subgroup and let
P=L\ltimes U
be a
\lambda_{0}
-stable Levi decomposition. The inclusion
\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}\subset\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}
and the projection
\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}\to\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}/\mathfrak{u}_{\eta}\cong\mathfrak{l}_{\eta}
yield a diagram of algebraic stacks

\displaystyle\left[L_{0}\backslash\mathfrak{l}_{\eta}\right]\xleftarrow{b}\left[P_{0}\backslash\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}\right]\xrightarrow{a}\left[G_{0}\backslash\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}\right].

The
\mathbf{Z}
-graded parabolic induction functor is defined by

\displaystyle\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}}=a_{*}\circ b^{*}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{L_{0,q}}(\mathfrak{l}_{\eta})\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{G_{0,q}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}).

The induction functor satisfies the transitivity : for every
\lambda_{0}
-stable parabolic subgroup
Q\subset L
, there is an isomorphisms of functors

\displaystyle\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}}\circ\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}_{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{\eta}}\cong\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}}_{(\mathfrak{q}\oplus\mathfrak{u})_{\eta}}.

A
cuspidal pair

(\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}
is a cuspidal pair on
\mathfrak{g}
such that
\mathrm{O}\cap\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}\neq\emptyset
. If
(\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
is a cuspidal pair, it happens that
\mathrm{O}_{\eta}=\mathrm{O}\cap\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}
must be the unique open
G_{0}
orbit in
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}
and for every
e\in\mathrm{O}_{\eta}
, the inclusion
Z_{G_{0}}(e)\subset Z_{G}(e)
induces an isomorphism on the component groups, see
[
16
, §4]
. It follows that the restriction
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}:=\mathscr{C}\mid_{\mathrm{O}}
is irreducible, and conversely,
\mathscr{C}
is the unique
G
-equivariant extension of
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
on
\mathrm{O}
. An
admissible system
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}
is a datum
(M,\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
of a
\lambda_{0}
-stable Levi subgroup
M\subset G
with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{m}=\Lie M
and a cuspidal pair
(\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
on
\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}
.

We shall analyse the extension algebra in the right-hand side of (
). For this sake, we introduce some combinatorial language for the Coxeter complex of
W
, so as to handle the varieties of fixed points
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{{\lambda_{q}}},\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{{\lambda_{q}}}
.

1.9
Coxeter complex and canonical relative Weyl group

Fix a datum
\xi=(M,\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
as in
subsection 1.1
. Choose a maximal torus
T\subset M_{0}
and set
\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{Q}}=\mathbf{X}_{*}(T)_{\mathbf{Q}}=\mathbf{X}_{*}(T)\otimes\mathbf{Q}
. Let
R=R(G,T)
denote the set of roots and let
W=N_{G}(T)/T
denote the Weyl group. The root hyperplanes decompose
\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{Q}}
into facets and the facets of maximal dimension are the Weyl chambers. Let
\mathfrak{F}
denote the set of facets (more precisely, the root hyperplanes yield a stratification of
\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{R}}=\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{R}
into facets and
\mathfrak{F}
is its restriction to
\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{Q}}\subset\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{R}}
). The Weyl group
W
acts on the set
\mathfrak{F}
. There is a bijection :

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}

\displaystyle\xlongrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle\left\{\text{parabolic subalgebra\penalty\ }\mathfrak{p}\subset\mathfrak{g}\;;\;\mathfrak{t}\subset\mathfrak{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}=\bigoplus_{r\geq 0}\prescript{y}{r}{\mathfrak{g}}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbf{X}^{*}(T)\\
\langle\alpha,y\rangle\geq 0\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}\qquad(y\in\sigma).

For every facet
\sigma\in\mathfrak{F}
, let
P^{\sigma}
denote the corresponding parabolic subgroup of
G
such that
\Lie P^{\sigma}=\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}
and
L^{\sigma}
its Levi factor containing
T
with
\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}=\Lie L^{\sigma}
.

The subspace
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}=\mathbf{X}_{*}(Z_{M})_{\mathbf{Q}}\subset\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{Q}}
is a union of facets in
\mathfrak{F}
and its pointwise stabiliser coincides with the Weyl group
W_{M}=W(M,T)\subset W
. The vanishing locus of each of the relative roots
\alpha\in R_{\xi}
gives a hyperplane in
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
. The root hyperplane arrangement of
R_{\xi}
coincides with the restriction to
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
of the root hyperplane arrangement of
R
. The
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
-chambers
\nu
are characterised by the property that
\mathfrak{l}^{\nu}=\mathfrak{m}
. Let
\Xi\subset\mathfrak{F}
be the subset of
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
-chambers. The normaliser
N_{W}(W_{M})
preserves
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
and the quotient
N_{W}(W_{M})/W_{M}
acts on
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
. It is shown in
[
12
, 5.9]
that
W_{\xi}\cong N_{W}(W_{M})/W_{M}
and it acts simply transitively on
\Xi
. Let
\Xi^{\prime}
be the set facets which are
W
-conjugate to some facet in
\Xi
. Consequently, the Weyl group
W
acts transitively on
\Xi^{\prime}
.

Each
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
-chamber
\nu\in\Xi
gives rise to a base
\Delta_{\xi}^{\nu}\subset R_{\xi}
. The base
\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi}
gives a set of Coxeter generators
\{s_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\Delta_{\xi}^{\nu}}
for
W_{\xi}
. We define the
canonical relative Weyl group
as
(\mathcal{W}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})=\varprojlim_{\nu}(W_{\xi},\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi})
. The elements of
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
can written in the form
w=(w^{\nu})_{\nu\in\Xi}
, so that for each
y\in W_{\xi}
and
\nu\in\Xi
we have
w^{y\nu}=yw^{\nu}y^{-1}
. It is a Coxeter group canonically attached to the admissible system
\xi
. We define also the canonical version of the based root system
\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\xi,\mathbf{Q}},\Delta_{\xi}\right)=\varprojlim_{\nu}(\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}},\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi})
, who has the Coxeter group
(\mathcal{W}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})
as Weyl group. In particular,
(\mathcal{W}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})
acts on
\mathfrak{h}_{\xi}
by orthogonal reflections.

We define a
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
-action on
\Xi
as follows :

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\xi}\times\Xi\to\Xi,\quad(w,\nu)\mapsto(w^{\nu})^{-1}\nu.

This action is simply transitive and commutes with the
W_{\xi}
-action on
\Xi
. Recall the point
\lambda^{M}\in\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
. It can be shown that
\lambda^{M}
is the image of
\lambda_{0}
under the orthogonal projection
\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{Q}}\to\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
with respect to the Killing form. Let
W_{\xi,\lambda^{M}}=\Stab_{W_{\xi}}(\lambda^{M})
. We denote
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}=W_{\xi,\lambda^{M}}\backslash\Xi
. The
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
-action on
\Xi
descends to
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}
. For every
\nu\in\Xi
, let
\lambda_{\nu}\in\mathfrak{h}_{\xi}
denote the image of
\lambda^{M}
under the isomorphism
(\mathfrak{h}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})\cong(\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}},\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi})
. There is a bijection of
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
-sets :

(1.10)

\displaystyle\underline{\smash{\Xi}}

\displaystyle\xlongrightarrow{\sim}\left\{\lambda_{\nu}\right\}_{\nu\in\Xi}

\displaystyle\underline{\smash{\nu}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\lambda_{\nu},

where
\underline{\smash{\nu}}
denotes the
W_{\xi,\lambda^{M}}
-orbit of some
\nu\in\Xi
.

The graded Hecke algebra
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}
from
subsection 1.1
can also be defined in terms of
(\mathfrak{h}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})
and it contains the polynomial subalgebra
\mathbf{S}_{\xi}=\mathbf{k}[\mathfrak{h}_{\xi}]=\Sym(\mathfrak{h}_{\xi}\otimes\mathbf{k})
as well as the group ring
\mathbf{C}\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
. It follows immediately that the possible
\mathbf{S}_{\xi}
-weights of modules in the block
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\mof_{(\lambda^{M},\eta)}
is
\{\lambda_{\nu}\}_{\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}
, which is in bijection with
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}
.

1.11
Description of the
{\lambda_{q}}
-fixed components

Recall
{\lambda_{q}}=(\lambda_{0},\eta/2)\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(M\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}})_{\mathbf{Q}}
. We choose a maximal torus
T\subset M_{0}
. It is easy to see that the
\lambda_{q}
-fixed point set
\mathfrak{g}^{{\lambda_{q}}}
coincides with the
\eta
-weight space
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}=\prescript{\lambda_{0}}{\eta}{\mathfrak{g}}
.

Consider the partial flag variety
\mathcal{P}=G/P
. From
subsection 1.9
, we know that there exists a unique
\nu_{0}\in\Xi
such that
\mathfrak{p}^{\nu_{0}}=\mathfrak{p}
. For any
\nu\in\Xi^{\prime}
, we have clearly
P^{\nu}=\dot{w}_{\nu}P\dot{w}^{-1}_{\nu}
for any
\dot{w}_{\nu}\in N_{G}(T)
such that its image
w_{\nu}\in W
satisfies
w_{\nu}\nu_{0}=\nu
. By
[
21
]
, the variety of
\lambda_{0}
-fixed points of
\mathcal{P}
has the following description as disjoint union of connected components :

(1.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\lambda_{0}}\cong\bigsqcup_{\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in W_{\lambda_{0}}\backslash\Xi^{\prime}}G_{0}/P^{\nu}_{0},\quad g\dot{w}_{\nu}P\mapsfrom gP^{\nu}_{0}\quad\text{for $g\in G_{0}$},

where
W_{\lambda_{0}}
is the stabiliser of
\lambda_{0}
in
W
,
P^{\nu}_{0}=(P^{\nu})^{\lambda_{0}}
and
\dot{w}_{\nu}\in N_{G}(T)
is as above.

For every facet
\nu\in\Xi^{\prime}
, put
\mathrm{O}^{\nu}=\Ad_{\dot{w}_{\nu}}\mathrm{O}\subset\mathfrak{l}^{\nu}
and
\mathrm{O}^{\nu}_{\eta}=\mathrm{O}^{\nu}\cap\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}
. Using the fibration
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}\to\mathcal{P}
, we derive from (
) a similar description for the variety of
{\lambda_{q}}
-fixed points of
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}
:

(1.13)

\displaystyle\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{{\lambda_{q}}}

\displaystyle\cong\bigsqcup_{\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in W_{\lambda_{0}}\backslash\Xi^{\prime}}\mathcal{T}^{\nu},\quad\mathcal{T}^{\nu}=G_{0}\times^{P^{\nu}_{0}}(\mathrm{O}^{\nu}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta})

\displaystyle(g\dot{w},\Ad_{\dot{w}^{-1}}(z))

\displaystyle\mapsfrom(g,z)\quad\text{for $g\in G_{0},\;z\in\mathrm{O}^{\nu}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{z}$}.

It is shown in
[
19
]
that
\mathrm{O}^{\nu}_{\eta}=\emptyset
if
\nu
is not
W_{\lambda_{0}}
-conjugate to any
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
-chamber and
\mathrm{O}^{\nu}=\mathrm{O}
if
\nu
is a
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
-chamber. In the former case, we have
\mathcal{T}^{\nu}=\emptyset
. Thus we are reduced to consider the subset
\Xi\subset\Xi^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
-chambers. The formula (
) can be rewritten as

(1.14)

\displaystyle\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{{\lambda_{q}}}\cong\bigsqcup_{\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\mathcal{T}^{\nu},\quad\mathcal{T}^{\nu}=G_{0}\times^{P^{\nu}_{0}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}).

For every facet
\sigma\in\mathfrak{F}
, we abbreviate
\Ind_{\sigma}=\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{\eta}}
for the parabolic
\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}
defined in
subsection 1.9
.

Proposition 1.15
.

The complex
a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\mathscr{C}
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\eta}
can be written in terms of parabolic inductions over graded Lie algebras :

\displaystyle a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}\cong\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\Ind_{\nu}\mathscr{C}_{\eta},

where we have identified
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
with its direct image under the inclusion
\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\subset\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}=\mathfrak{l}^{\nu}_{\eta}
for
\nu\in\Xi
.

Démonstration.

It follows from (
) that

\displaystyle a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}\cong\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\Ind_{\nu}\left(\Ad_{\dot{w}^{-1}_{\nu}}^{*}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}\right),

where
\Ad_{\dot{w}^{-1}_{\nu}}:\mathrm{O}\to\mathrm{O}
is the adjoint action of
\dot{w}^{-1}_{\nu}
on
\mathfrak{m}
. As mentioned in
subsection 1.1
, the cuspidal local system
\mathscr{C}
has a
N_{G}(Z^{\circ}_{M})
equivariant structure. Since
\nu\in\Xi
, we must have
\dot{w}_{\nu}\in N_{G}(Z^{\circ}_{M})
. The equivariance yields an isomorphism
\Ad^{*}_{\dot{w}^{-1}_{\nu}}\mathscr{C}\cong\mathscr{C}
, which restricts to
\Ad^{*}_{\dot{w}^{-1}_{\nu}}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}\cong\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
.
∎

1.16
Completion

Recall that the canonical relative Weyl group
(\mathcal{W}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})
acts on the vector space
\mathfrak{h}_{\xi}
. We have the classical Chern–Weil isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Sym(\mathbf{X}^{*}(T\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}})_{\mathbf{k}})^{W}\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q}},\quad\Sym(\mathbf{X}^{*}(T\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}})_{\mathbf{k}})^{W_{\lambda_{0}}}\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{0,q}}.

Let
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q},\lambda_{q}}
(resp.
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{0,q},0}
) denote the completion of the ring
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q}}
(resp.
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{0,q}}
) at the maximal ideal generated by
f-f(\lambda_{q})
for
f\in\Sym(\mathbf{X}^{*}(T\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}})_{\mathbf{k}})^{W}
(resp. at the augmentation ideal
\mathrm{H}^{>0}_{G_{0,q}}
). There is an isomorphism
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q},\lambda_{q}}\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{0,q},0}
which respects the adic topology on both sides.

For any coherent
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q}}
-module
\mathscr{M}
(resp. coherent
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{0,q}}
-module
\mathscr{M}
), denote

\mathscr{M}_{\lambda_{q}}=\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q}}}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q},\lambda_{q}}\quad\text{(resp. $\mathscr{M}_{0}=\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{0,q}}}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{0,q},0}$)}.

By
subsection 1.11
, the homomorphism (
) can be written as

\displaystyle\Phi:\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\to\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}},\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\Ext^{*}_{G_{0,q}}\left(\Ind_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}_{\eta},\Ind_{\nu}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}\right)_{0}=:\widehat{\mathcal{H}}.

This homomorphism satisfies the following remarkable properties :

—

If
\mathscr{M}\in\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\mof_{(\lambda^{M},\eta/2)}
, then the
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}
-module structure on
\mathscr{M}
factors through
\Phi
.

—

For each
\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}
, the composition

\mathbf{S}_{\xi}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\xrightarrow{\Phi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}\to\Ext^{*}_{G_{0,q}}\left(\Ind_{\nu}\mathscr{C}_{\eta},\Ind_{\nu}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}\right)/\mathrm{H}^{>0}_{G_{0,q}}=:\mathscr{N}

makes
\mathscr{N}
into a left (resp. right)
\mathbf{S}_{\xi}
-module supported on eigenvalue
(\lambda_{\nu},\eta/2)\in\mathfrak{h}_{\xi,\mathbf{k}}\times\mathbf{k}
.

The second point gives a geometric meaning to the combinatorial bijection (
) ; in other words, for each
\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}
, the eigenvalue
(\lambda_{\nu},\eta/2)
of the
\mathbf{S}_{\xi}
-action is accounted for by the connected component
\mathcal{T}^{\nu}\subset\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\lambda_{q}}
.

1.17
Compatibility with parabolic induction

Let
J\subset\Delta_{\xi}
be a subset. Denote by
\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J}\subset\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
the parabolic subgroup generated by
\{s_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in J}
. For any
\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J}
-orbit
S\in\Xi/\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J}
, there is a unique
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
-facet
\sigma
such that
\overline{\sigma}=\bigcap_{\nu\in S}\overline{\nu}
. Let
\Xi_{J}\subset\mathfrak{F}
denote the set of facets
\sigma
arising in this way. Then each
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
-facet belongs to
\Xi_{J}
for some
J\subseteq\Delta_{\xi}
. Conversely, for each
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
-facet
\sigma\in\Xi_{J}
, we denote by
\Xi^{\sigma}\subset\Xi
the subset of
\Xi
consisting of
\mathfrak{z}_{M,\mathbf{Q}}
-chambers whose closure contains
\sigma
. Then
\Xi^{\sigma}
forms a
\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J}
-orbit in
\Xi
. This yields a bijection of
W_{\xi}
-sets
\Xi/\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J}\cong\Xi_{J}
for each
J\subset\Delta_{\xi}
.

As before, we denote
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}_{J}=W_{\xi,\lambda^{M}}\backslash\Xi_{J}
and
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\sigma}=W_{\xi,\sigma,\lambda^{M}}\backslash\Xi^{\sigma}
, where
W_{\xi,\sigma,\lambda^{M}}=\Stab_{W_{\xi}}(\sigma,\lambda^{M})
is the pointwise stabiliser of
\sigma
and
\lambda^{M}
. We denote by
\underline{\smash{\sigma}}
the image of
\sigma
in
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}/\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J}
.

We define
P^{\nu\leq\sigma}=L^{\sigma}\cap P^{\nu}
for any
\nu\in\Xi^{\sigma}
, so that
P^{\nu\leq\sigma}
is a parabolic subgroup of
L^{\sigma}
with Levi factor
L^{\nu}=M
. For simplifying the notation, we abbreviate

\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu}=\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma\leq\nu}_{\eta}}.

The map (
) for the Levi subgroup
L^{\sigma}
and the admissible system
\xi
on
\mathfrak{l}_{\eta}
is the following :

\Phi_{\sigma}:\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi,J}\to\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}},\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\sigma}}\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{0,q}}\left(\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}_{\eta},\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}\right)_{0}=:\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma},

where the subscript
0
means the completion at the augmentation ideal
\mathrm{H}^{>0}_{L^{\sigma}_{0,q}}
. Taking product over all classes
\underline{\smash{\sigma}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}_{J}
, we obtain a homomorphism :

\displaystyle\Phi_{J}=\left(\Phi_{\sigma}\right)_{\underline{\smash{\sigma}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}_{J}}:\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi,J}\to\prod_{\underline{\smash{\sigma}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}_{J}}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}.

On the other hand, the functoriality of the functor
\Ind_{\sigma}
and the transitivity of parabolic inductions
\Ind_{\sigma}\circ\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu}\cong\Ind_{\nu}
yields a map

\psi_{J}:\prod_{\underline{\smash{\sigma}}}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}\to\prod_{\underline{\smash{\sigma}}}\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}},\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\sigma}}\Ext^{*}_{G_{0,q}}\left(\Ind_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}_{\eta},\Ind_{\nu}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}\right)_{0}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{H}}.

Proposition 1.18
.

The construction of
\Phi
is compatible with parabolic induction in the sense that
\Phi\mid_{\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi,J}}=\psi_{J}\circ\Phi_{J}
.

Démonstration.

Recall the usual (ungraded) parabolic induction functor : for each parabolic subgroup
P\subseteq G
with Levi factor
M
, the diagram

[\mathfrak{m}/M]\xleftarrow{b}[\mathfrak{p}/P]\xrightarrow{a}[\mathfrak{g}/G]

gives the induction functor
\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}:=a_{*}b^{*}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{M_{q}}(\mathfrak{m}^{\mathrm{nil}})\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{G_{q}}(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}})
. By the
N_{G}(Z^{\circ}_{M})
-equivariance (
subsection 1.1
) of
\mathscr{C}
, for any other parabolic
P^{\prime}
having
M
as Levi factor, there is a canonical choice of isomorphism
\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}\cong\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathscr{C}
; thus we may write
\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C}:=\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathscr{C}
. Similarly, for any
\sigma\in\Xi_{J}
, we may write
\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C}:=\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma\leq\nu}}\mathscr{C}
by choosing an arbitrary
\nu\in\Xi^{\sigma}
.

Choose any
\sigma\in\Xi_{J}
and
\nu\in\Xi^{\sigma}
. It follows from Lusztig’s construction
[
13
]
,
[
14
]
of isomorphism
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}\cong\Ext^{*}_{G_{q}}(a_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},a_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}})
that the following diagram commutes

(1.19)

\displaystyle\hbox to424.58pt{\vbox to75.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 212.28633pt\lower-37.05452pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Denote the composite map of the top-right corner by

(1.20)

\displaystyle\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}}:\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C})\to\Ext^{*}_{G_{q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C}).

For each
\sigma\in\Xi_{J}
, applying
subsection 1.17
below and taking conjugation by Weyl group elements, we obtain

\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C})\otimes_{\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q}}}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q},\lambda_{q}}\cong\prod_{\underline{\smash{\sigma}}^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}_{J}}\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C})_{\lambda_{q}}.

Completing the both sides of (
) at
\lambda_{q}
over

Lemma 1.21
.

For each
\sigma\in\Xi_{J}
, there is an isomorphism

\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C})\otimes_{\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q}}}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q},\lambda_{q}}\cong\prod_{w}\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C})_{w^{-1}\lambda_{q}},

where
w
runs over a complete set of representatives for
W_{\xi,\lambda^{M}}\backslash W_{\xi}/W_{\xi,\sigma}
in
W_{\xi}
.

Démonstration.

Since
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{L^{\sigma}}\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{P^{\sigma}}\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G}(G/P^{\sigma},\mathbf{k})
, applying the equivariant localisation, we obtain

(1.22)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q}}\otimes_{\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q}}}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q},\lambda_{q}}

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G^{\lambda_{0}}_{q}}((G/P^{\sigma})^{\lambda_{0}},\mathbf{k})_{\lambda_{q}}\cong\prod_{w}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G^{\lambda_{0}}_{q}}((G^{\lambda_{0}}/({\rm Phys.\penalty\ Rev.\penalty\ E}{w}P^{\sigma}))^{\lambda_{0}},\mathbf{k})_{\lambda_{q}}

\displaystyle\cong\prod_{w}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{(L^{w\sigma}_{q})^{\lambda_{0}},\lambda_{q}}\cong\prod_{w}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{L^{w\sigma}_{q},\lambda_{q}}\cong\prod_{w}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q},w^{-1}\lambda_{q}},

where
w
runs over a complete set of representatives for
W_{\lambda_{0}}\backslash W/W_{\sigma}
in
W=W(G,T)
. Taking tensor product of
\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C})
with (
), we obtain

\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C})\otimes_{\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q}}}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{G_{q},\lambda_{q}}\cong\prod_{w}\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C})_{w^{-1}\lambda_{q}}.

It remains to show that for each
w\in W
such that
W_{\lambda_{0}}wW_{\sigma}\cap N_{W}(W_{M})=\emptyset
, the corresponding factor in this product vanishes. It is known (see
[
13
, 4.3]
or
subsection 7.2
below) that, as
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q}}
-module, the cohomology
\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C})
is supported on in image of the following map

(1.23)

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{*}(Z^{q}_{M}(\phi))_{\mathbf{k}}\to\mathbf{X}_{*}(T_{q})_{\mathbf{k}}/W_{\sigma}\cong\Spec\mathrm{H}^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q}},

where
Z^{q}_{M}(\phi)\subset M_{q}
is defined in the same way as in
subsection 1.3
for any choice of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple
\phi
. Thus
w^{-1}\lambda_{q}
lies in this support if and only if there exists
y\in W_{\sigma}
such that
yw^{-1}\lambda_{q}\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(Z^{q}_{M}(\phi))_{\mathbf{Q}}
.

Suppose that
w\in W
is such that
w^{-1}\lambda_{q}
lies in the image of (
). Since
W_{\xi}
acts transitively on
\Xi
, there exists
v\in W_{\xi}
such that
v\lambda_{q}=yw^{-1}\lambda_{q}
. Since
W_{\xi}\cong N_{W}(W_{M})/W_{M}
, we may choose any lifting
\dot{v}\in N_{W}(W_{M})
of
v
, we see that
y^{\prime}:=\dot{v}^{-1}yw^{-1}\in W_{\lambda_{0}}
. Therefore in this case
\dot{v}^{-1}=y^{\prime}wy^{-1}\in W_{\lambda_{0}}wW_{\sigma}\cap N_{W}(W_{M})\neq\emptyset
. It follows that the condition
W_{\lambda_{0}}wW_{\sigma}\cap N_{W}(W_{M})=\emptyset
implies
\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mathscr{C})_{w^{-1}\lambda_{q}}=0
, which concludes the proof.
∎

2
Outline of the strategy

In this section, we explain the strategy to prove the main theorem. The discussion here will be informal and serve as guideline for the sections after.

2.1
Springer correspondence for dDAHAs : heuristics

Let
G
be a simply connected simple algebraic group over
\mathbf{C}
with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
. Consider the loop group
G_{\aff}=G(\!(\varpi)\!)
and the loop Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}=\mathfrak{g}(\!(\varpi)\!)
. Let
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}=\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be the multiplicative group which acts on the ring of formal Laurent series
\mathbf{C}(\!(\varpi)\!)
by
(t,\varpi)\mapsto t\varpi
. It induces a
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-action on
G_{\aff}
and on
\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}
. Let
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}=\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be the multiplicative group which acts on
\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}
by weight
-2
and trivially on
G_{\aff}
. For any
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-stable subgroup
G^{\prime}\subset G_{\aff}
, we denote
G^{\prime}_{\diamond}=G^{\prime}\rtimes{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
and
G^{\prime}_{\diamond,q}=G^{\prime}_{\diamond}\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
. Hereafter, the subscript
\diamond
will indicate the presence of the loop-rotation torus
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
.

Suppose that we have a
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-stable parahoric subgroup
P\subset G_{\aff}
with
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-stable Levi factor
M\subset P
and an
M_{\diamond}
-equivariant cuspidal local system
\mathscr{C}
on a nilpotent
M_{\diamond}
-orbit
\mathrm{O}\subset\mathfrak{m}^{\mathrm{nil}}
. Denote by
\pi:\mathfrak{p}\to\mathfrak{m}
the projection. One would like to perform the same constructions on the extension algebra of the induced complex
\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathscr{C}
on the loop Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}
. Imitating the case of graded affine Hecke algebras, one defines the partial Springer resolution

\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}=G_{\aff}\times^{P}\pi^{-1}(\mathrm{O})\xrightarrow{a}\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}

and extends
\mathscr{C}
to a
G_{\aff,\diamond,q}
-equivariant local system
\dot{\mathscr{C}}
on
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}
. Fix such a system
\xi=(G_{\aff},P,M,\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
. One would hope to carry out the same constructions as
section 1
in this affine context with
\xi
.

Let
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}=\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}\times_{\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}}\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}
and let
p_{1},p_{2}:\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}\to\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}
be the projections. By the Verdier duality, the extension algebra
\Ext^{*}_{G_{\aff,\diamond,q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathscr{C})
would then be isomorphic to
\Ext^{*}_{G_{\aff,\diamond,q}}(p_{1}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},p_{2}^{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}})
equipped with an appropriate convolution product. One would then need to construct a ring homomorphism from the dDAHA
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
(to be defined in
subsection 5.1
) to the convolution algebra
\Ext^{*}_{G_{\aff,\diamond,q}}(p_{1}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},p_{2}^{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}})
.

Let
\phi=(e,h,f)
be a
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple in
\mathfrak{m}
such that
e\in\mathrm{O}
and let
Z^{q}_{M_{\diamond}}(\phi)
be the group defined in (
) so that there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\iota:Z_{M_{\diamond}}\times\mathbf{C}^{\times}\cong Z^{q}_{M_{\diamond}}(\phi)

\displaystyle(g,q)\mapsto(gq^{h},q).

Fix
\lambda^{M}\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(Z_{M_{\diamond}})
and
\eta\in\mathbf{Z}_{\neq 0}
. Write
{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}=\iota_{*}(\lambda^{M},\eta/2)\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(Z^{q}_{M_{\diamond}}(\phi))_{\mathbf{Q}}
. Let
\delta\in\mathbf{X}^{*}(G_{\aff,\diamond})
denote the projection
G_{\aff,\diamond}\to{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
and let
m=\delta(\lambda^{M})\in\mathbf{Z}
. We assume that
\eta\neq 0
and
m>0
.
†
†

†

The condition
m\neq 0
is to ensure that the fixed components are algebraic varieties and is crucial in our approach. The condition
\eta\neq 0
is not essential but the case
\eta=0
cannot be treated uniformly. For this reason we exclude it.

One considers the following diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}}_{\aff}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}^{{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}}_{\aff}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\rho}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}=G_{\aff}\times^{P}\pi^{-1}(\mathrm{O})\xrightarrow{a}\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}
: affine partial Springer resolution

\mathfrak{g}^{{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}}_{\aff},\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}}_{\aff}
:
\lambda_{q}
-fixed points

\rho,\dot{\rho}
: inclusions of
\lambda_{q}
-fixed points

Finally, one would hope to apply the equivariant localisation to
\Ext^{*}_{G_{\aff,\diamond,q}}(p_{1}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},p_{2}^{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}})
to show that

\Ext^{*}_{G_{\aff,\diamond,q}}(a_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},a_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}})_{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}\cong\Ext^{*}_{G^{{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}}_{\aff,\diamond,q}}\left(a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}\right)_{0}

and to obtain a injective ring homomorphism with dense image :

(2.2)

\displaystyle\Phi:\mathbf{H}_{\xi}\to\Ext^{*}_{G^{{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}}_{\aff,\diamond,q}}\left(a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}\right)_{0}.

This would yield an analogue of
subsection 1.3
for the dDAHA
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
.

However, we will not construct the homomorphism
\Phi
in (
) from an isomorphism
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}\cong\Ext^{*}_{G_{\aff,\diamond,q}}\left(a_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}},a_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}\right)
by means of localisation. This is because there is no straightforward implementation of
G_{\aff,\diamond,q}
-equivariant cohomology for the reason that
G_{\aff}
is not an algebraic group. Instead, we shall work directly with the varieties of
{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}
-fixed points
\mathfrak{g}^{{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}}_{\aff}
and
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}}_{\aff}
. Under the assumptions that we made about the cocharacter
{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}
, these varieties are disjoint unions of algebraic varieties of finite type, which can be described in terms of cyclic grading on
\mathfrak{g}
and
spirals
and
splittings
introduced by Lusztig–Yun in
[
18
]
. Moreover, the connected components of
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}}_{\aff}
have a description similar to the varieties
\mathcal{T}^{\nu}
appearing in
subsection 1.11
.

2.3
\mathbf{Z}
-graded loop Lie algebras versus
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graded Lie algebras

We briefly discuss here how to reduce the problem of the study of nilpotent cone of a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded affine Lie algebra to the one of a
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graded simple Lie algebra for
m=\delta(\lambda^{M})\in\mathbf{Z}_{>0}
as above.

Choose a
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-stable maximal torus
T\subset M
and denote by
R=R(G,T)
the root system. Put
T_{\diamond}=T\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
.
The affine Weyl group
W
of
G_{\aff}
is given by
W=N_{G_{\aff,\diamond}}(T_{\diamond})/T_{\diamond}
. It acts linearly on the cocharacter lattice
\mathbf{X}_{*}(T_{\diamond})
.
Write
{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}=(\lambda_{0},m,\eta/2)\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(T\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}})
and denote
{\lambda_{\diamond}}=(\lambda_{0},m)
. Then the weight spaces of
\lambda_{\diamond}
yields a
\mathbf{Z}
-grading on
\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}

\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\mathfrak{g}_{\aff,n}\;,\quad\mathfrak{g}_{\aff,n}:=\prescript{\lambda_{\diamond}}{n}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbf{Z}}\left\{z\otimes\varpi^{k}\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes\varpi^{k}\;;\;\lambda_{0}(t)z=t^{n-km}z,\quad\forall t\in\mathbf{C}^{\times}\right\}.

On the other hand, the restriction
\theta:=\lambda_{0}\mid_{\mu_{m}}:\mu_{m}\to T
yields a
\mathbf{Z}/m
-grading

\mathfrak{g}=\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{n}}\in\mathbf{Z}/m}\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{n}}}\;,\quad\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{n}}}:=\prescript{\theta}{\underline{\smash{n}}}{\mathfrak{g}}=\left\{z\in\mathfrak{g}\;;\;\lambda_{0}(\zeta)z=\zeta^{n}z,\quad\forall\zeta\in\mu_{m}\right\}.

Then we have an isomorphism on graded pieces induced by the evaluation
\varpi\mapsto 1
:

(2.4)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\aff,n}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{n}}},\quad\forall n\in\mathbf{Z}.

Similarly, we can deduce that the evaluation
\varpi\mapsto 1
yields

(2.5)

\displaystyle G_{\aff,0}\cong G_{\underline{\smash{0}}},\quad\text{where $G_{\aff,0}:=G_{\aff}^{\lambda_{\diamond}}$ and $G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}:=G^{\theta}$.}

Moreover for each
n\in\mathbf{Z}
, the adjoint action of
G_{\aff,0}
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\aff,n}
is isomorphic to the adjoint action of
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{n}}}
.

2.6
Parahorics and spirals

Let
P\subset G_{\aff}
be a
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-stable parahoric subgroup containing
T
and denote
\mathfrak{p}=\Lie P
. We can find a cocharacter
\mu_{\diamond}=(\mu,m^{\prime})\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(T)\oplus\mathbf{Z}_{>0}\subset\mathbf{X}_{*}({T_{\diamond}})
such that
\mathfrak{p}=\prescript{\mu_{\diamond}}{\geq 0}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}
. Put
\mathfrak{p}_{n}=\mathfrak{p}\cap\mathfrak{g}_{\aff,n}
and
\mathfrak{l}_{n}=\mathfrak{l}\cap\mathfrak{g}_{\aff,n}
for
n\in\mathbf{Z}
and put
P_{0}=P^{\lambda_{\diamond}}
and
L_{0}=L^{\lambda_{\diamond}}
. Via the isomorphism (
), we can describe
\mathfrak{p}_{n}
alternatively as subspace of
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{n}}}
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{n}\cong\prescript{m^{\prime}\lambda_{0}-m\mu}{\geq m^{\prime}n}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\underline{\smash{n}}}.

This description gives rise to the notion of a
spiral
, introduced in
[
18
]
as the
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graded counterpart of a parahoric subalgebra. The cocharacter
\mu_{\diamond}
also gives rise to a
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-stable Levi factor of
P
is denoted by
L=G^{\mu_{\diamond}}_{\aff}
. The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{l}=\Lie L
can be described alternatively by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{l}_{n}\cong\prescript{m^{\prime}\lambda_{0}-m\mu}{m^{\prime}n}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\underline{\smash{n}}}.

Similarly, one can regard
P_{0},L_{0}
and
L
as algebraic subgroups of
G
such that
\Lie P_{0}=\mathfrak{p}_{0}
,
\Lie L_{0}=\mathfrak{l}_{0}
and
\Lie L=\mathfrak{l}
. The notion of spirals will be reviewed in
section 3
.

2.7
Fixed-point components of
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}
and
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}

We suppose that there is a
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-stable parahoric subgroup
P\subset G_{\aff}
with a
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-stable Levi factor
M\subset P
which contains
T
. Suppose also that there is a nilpotent orbit
\mathrm{O}\subset\mathfrak{m}^{\mathrm{nil}}
such that
\mathrm{O}\cap\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}\neq\emptyset
which carries an
M
-equivariant cuspidal local system
\mathscr{C}
. As before, the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{m}=\Lie M
is
\mathbf{Z}
-graded by the weights of
\lambda_{\diamond}
. Such a datum
\xi=(M,\mathfrak{m}_{*},\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
is called an
admissible system
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
, see
subsection 3.4
. Given an admissible system
\xi=(M,\mathfrak{m}_{*},\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
, just as in the case of reductive group, one can define a relative affine root system (
subsection 4.1
) and a relative affine Weyl group
W_{\xi}
.

The sets
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}=G\times^{P}\pi^{-1}(\mathrm{O})\to\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}
and
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}
have a
G_{\aff,\diamond,q}
-action induced from the actions on
G_{\aff}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}
. As in the case of finite reductive groups
subsection 1.11
, the
{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}
-fixed points of this action in
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}
can be described as a disjoint union of connected components

\displaystyle\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}_{\aff}\cong\coprod_{\nu\in W_{\xi,\lambda}\backslash\Xi}\mathcal{T}^{\nu}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}},

where
\Xi
is the affine analogue of the set introduced in
subsection 1.9
, which carries a
W_{\xi}
-action and
W_{\xi,\lambda}=\Stab_{W_{\xi}}(\lambda^{M})
. We will define the set
\Xi
in
subsection 4.13
and the variety
\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
in
subsection 6.17
. Put
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}=W_{\xi,\lambda}\backslash\Xi
.

Since we have
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}_{\aff}=\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}_{\aff}\times_{\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}}\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}_{\aff}
, there is a similar description for the Steinberg variety :

(2.8)

\displaystyle\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}_{\aff}\cong\coprod_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\quad\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=\mathcal{T}^{\nu}\times_{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}.

In consequence, the fixed-point sets
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}
,
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}
are disjoint unions of
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}
-equivariant algebraic varieties and the formalism of six operations and perverse sheaves is at our disposition.

2.9
Outline of the proof

We can now explain the strategy of the construction of the homomorphism

\displaystyle\Phi:\mathbf{H}_{\xi}\to\prod_{\nu^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\bigoplus_{\nu\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\Ext^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathbf{I}^{\nu^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{\nu}\right)_{0}.

Step 1

We recall in
section 3
the basic constructions on a
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graded Lie algebra introduced in
[
18
]
, such as the spiral induction functor
\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}}
and admissible systems on the nilpotent cone. It is a
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graded analogue of
subsection 1.8
.

Step 2

The
\mathbf{Z}/m
-counterpart of
subsection 1.9
is introduced in
section 4
. We recall there the affine analogue of the facet decomposition
\mathfrak{F}
of an affine space
{\mathbb{A}}
and the relative affine hyperplane
\mathbb{E}^{M}
, introduced in
[
19
]
. We can identify
\mathbb{E}^{M}
with the space
\mathbf{X}_{*}(Z_{M_{\diamond}})_{\mathbf{Q}}
of fractional cocharacters. There is a bijection

\displaystyle\Xi=\left\{\text{$\mathbb{E}^{M}$-chamber}\right\}

\displaystyle\xlongrightarrow{\sim}\left\{\mathfrak{p}_{*}\text{ spiral}\;;\;\mathfrak{m}_{*}\subset\mathfrak{p}_{*}\penalty\ \text{ is a splitting}\right\}

\displaystyle\nu

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{*}

Then, we define the relative affine root system
(\mathbb{E}^{M},R_{\xi})
as well as its Weyl group
W_{\xi}
. Each element
\nu\in\Xi
yields a base
\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi}\subset R_{\xi}
. We introduce also the canonical based relative root system
(\mathbb{E}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})
and the canonical relative Weyl group
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
. These two groups
W_{\xi}
and
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
act simply transitively on
\Xi
and their action commute. We define also the linearisation
\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}
of
\mathbb{E}_{\xi}
.

Step 4

We put
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}=W_{\xi,\lambda}\backslash\Xi
. There is a bijection between
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}
and the set of connected components of
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}
.
Following
[
19
]
, for each
\nu\in\Xi
, consider the corresponding spiral
\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{*}
and the spiral induction
\mathbf{I}^{\nu}=\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{\eta}}\mathscr{C}\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)
. We
realise
the complex
a_{*}\dot{\rho}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}
as the infinite sum
\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\mathbf{I}^{\nu}
. Put

\widehat{\mathcal{H}}=\prod_{\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\Ext^{*}_{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}(\mathbf{I}^{\nu^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{\nu})_{0}.

Step 5

Let
J\subsetneq\Delta_{\xi}
and consider the parabolic subalgebra
\mathbf{H}_{\xi,J}\subset\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
. For each
\sigma\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}/\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J}
, there is an extension algebra

\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}=\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}},\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\sigma}}\Ext^{*}_{H_{0,\diamond,q}}(\Ind^{\mathfrak{h}_{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{q}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\mathfrak{h}_{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{q}^{\nu}_{\eta}}\mathscr{C})_{0},

where
\mathfrak{h}_{*}=\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{*}
is a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded pseudo-Levi subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{q}^{\nu}_{*}=\mathfrak{h}_{*}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{*}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{*}
is the
\mathbf{Z}
-graded parabolic subalgebra corresponding to
\nu
. The construction of
[
13
]

[
14
]

[
3
]
yields a homomorphism
\Phi_{\sigma}:\mathbf{H}_{\xi,J}\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}
. Taking product over
\sigma
, we obtain

\Phi_{J}=\left(\Phi_{\sigma}\right)_{\underline{\smash{\sigma}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}/\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J}}:\mathbf{H}_{\xi,J}\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{J}=\prod_{\underline{\smash{\sigma}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}/\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J}}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}

The functoriality of
\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{\eta}}
yields an homomorphism
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{J}\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
, which is injective.

Step 6

For
K\subset J\subsetneq\Delta_{\xi}
, the compatibility result
subsection 1.17
implies that there is a commutative square :

\hbox to97.1pt{\vbox to46.72pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 48.54997pt\lower-22.40277pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
