This implies that we can take the direct limit over the partially ordered set of subsets
\left(\{J\subsetneq\Delta_{\xi}\},\subseteq\right)
and obtain

\displaystyle\Phi=\varinjlim_{J}\Phi_{J}:\varinjlim_{J}\mathbf{H}_{\xi,J}\to\varinjlim_{J}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{J}\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}.

Using the fact that
\varinjlim_{J}\mathbf{H}_{\xi,J}=\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
, we obtain the map
\Phi
.

In the case where
J=\emptyset
, we have the smallest parabolic subalgebra
\mathbf{H}_{\xi,\emptyset}\subset\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
, which is equal to a polynomial ring
\mathbf{S}_{\xi}=\mathbf{k}[\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}]\otimes\mathbf{k}[u]
.
The second objective
section 8
is to show that the image of
\Phi
is dense in
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
when the latter is equipped with a suitable topology. There are two essential ingredients :

(i)

There is an affine version of the correspondence
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}\longleftrightarrow\left\{\lambda_{\nu}\right\}
. This will allow to show that the restriction of
\Phi
to the polynomial part
\mathbf{S}_{\xi}
contains all the idempotents
(\mathbf{e}_{\nu})_{\nu\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}
in
subsection 1.16
. It is done in
subsection 8.2
.

(ii)

An analysis of the Steinberg varieties
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}:=\mathcal{T}^{\nu}\times_{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}
by stratification is done in
section 6
.

(iii)

A filtration on
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
by the Bruhat order is defined in
subsection 7.7
. The associated quotient of this filtration can be studied using the stratification on the Steinberg varieties. We deduce a surjectivity result (
subsection 7.10
) which is crucial in the proof of the density theorem (
section 8
).

(iv)

For each
J
, the map
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{J}\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
is studied determined in
subsection 7.17
.

Remark 2.10
.

The module category of
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
in which we are interested is, by convention, the category
\mathcal{O}(\mathbf{H}_{\xi,\delta=1})
consisting of finitely generated
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
-modules on which the polynomial part
\mathbf{S}_{\xi}
acts locally finitely and the imaginary root
\delta\in\mathbb{E}^{*}_{\xi,\diamond}
acts by
1
. However, we have
\delta(\lambda^{M})=\delta({\lambda_{\diamond}})=m
. For this reason we need to renormalise the parameters by setting
\mathbf{x}^{M}=\lambda^{M}/m
,
\mathbf{x}=\lambda_{\diamond}/m
,
\mathbf{x}_{\nu}=\lambda_{\nu}/m
. We will consider the block
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{x}_{\nu},\eta/2m}(\mathbf{H}_{\xi,\delta=1})
consisting of modules
\mathscr{M}\in\mathcal{O}(\mathbf{H}_{\xi,\delta=1})
on which
\mathbf{S}_{\xi}/(\delta-1)
acts with eigenvalues in the orbit
W_{\xi}\mathbf{x}_{\nu}\subset\mathbb{E}_{\xi}
, see
subsection 9.4
.

3
\mathbf{Z}/m
-grading, spirals and splittings

We recall in this section some of the basic constructions over
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graded Lie algebras introduced in
[
18
]
and
[
19
]
.

3.1
\mathbf{Z}/m
-grading on
G

Let
G
be a connected simply connected simple algebraic group over
\mathbf{C}
. The Lie algebra is denoted
\mathfrak{g}=\Lie G
.

We fix a positive integer
m\in\mathbf{Z}_{>0}
. For any integer
k\in\mathbf{Z}
, we denote
\underline{\smash{k}}=k\mod m\in\mathbf{Z}/m
. Let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}=\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{i}}\in\mathbf{Z}/m}\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{i}}}

be a
\mathbf{Z}/m
-grading on
\mathfrak{g}
such that
\left[\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{i}}},\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{j}}}\right]\subseteq\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{i}}+\underline{\smash{j}}}
for all
\underline{\smash{i}},\underline{\smash{j}}\in\mathbf{Z}/m
. Let
\mu_{m}\subset\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be the group of
m
-th roots of unity. We define a homomorphism
\theta:\mu_{m}\to\Aut(\mathfrak{g})
by setting

\displaystyle\theta(\zeta)\mid_{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{j}}}}=\zeta^{j},\quad\forall\zeta\in\mu_{m}\;\forall j\in\mathbf{Z}.

The simplicity and the simple connectedness of
G
imply that
\Aut(G)\cong\Aut(\mathfrak{g})
, so we can write
\theta:\mu_{m}\to\Aut(G)
. The fixed-point subgroup
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}=G^{\theta}\subset G
is connected by the theorem of Steinberg
[
23
, 8.1]
.

We assume that the
\mathbf{Z}/m
-grading is inner
‡
‡

‡

This assumption is made in order to simplify the presentation below. The affine root system
R_{\aff}
that we introduce in
below, under this assumption, is
untwisted
(denoted
A^{(1)}_{n},B^{(1)}_{n},\ldots
in Kac’s notation). We indicate in
how to adjust the construction to the case where
\theta
is not inner.
in the sense that
\theta(\mu_{m})\subset G^{\ad}
, where
G^{\ad}
is the adjoint group of
G
. Under this assumption, we may assume without loss of generality that
\theta
admits a lifting
\mu_{m}\to G
— indeed, we may pre-compose
\theta
with a finite cover
[m^{\prime}]:\mu_{m^{\prime}m}\to\mu_{m}
, where
m^{\prime}=\#Z_{G}
, so that the resulting homomorphism
\theta^{\prime}:\mu_{m^{\prime}m}\to G^{\ad}
lifts to
G
; under this change, the grading on
\mathfrak{g}
is multiplied by
m^{\prime}
. There exists then a cocharacter
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbf{X}_{*}\left(G\right)
such that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{i}}}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbf{Z}\\
\underline{\smash{i}}=\underline{\smash{k}}\end{subarray}}\prescript{\lambda_{0}}{k}{\mathfrak{g}}.

We fix once and for all the choice of such a cocharacter
\lambda_{0}
.

Besides, we fix a maximal torus
T\subseteq G
which centralises
\lambda_{0}
so that
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbf{X}_{*}\left(T\right)
. In particular,
T
is contained in
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
. Fix once and for all an integer
\eta\in\mathbf{Z}_{\neq 0}
. Let
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}^{\mathrm{nil}}=\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}
be the closed subvariety of nilpotent elements.

3.2
Jacobson–Morosov theorem

Recall the theorem of Jacobson–Morosov in the
\mathbf{Z}/m
-graded setting. Let
e\in\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}^{\mathrm{nil}}
. According to
[
18
, 2.3]
, we can complete
e
into an
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple
\phi=\left(e,h,f\right)
with
h\in\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
and
f\in\mathfrak{g}_{-\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
. Consequently, there is a cocharacter of
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}
, the exponentiation of
h
, which we denote by
\exp(h):t\mapsto t^{h}
. Moreover, the set of such triple
\phi
with a given
e
forms a principal homogeneous space under the action of the unipotent part of the stabiliser of
e
in
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
. Using the Jacobson–Morosov theorem, one can show that the
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
-orbits in
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
are invariant under homothety.

3.3
Spirals, nilpotent radical and splittings

Let
\mu\in\mathbf{X}_{*}\left(G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\right)_{\mathbf{Q}}
be a fractional cocharacter and let
\varepsilon\in\{1,-1\}
. We attach to it a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded Lie algebra
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mu}_{*}=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mu}_{n}
where

\displaystyle\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mu}_{n}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in\mathbf{Q}\\
r\geq\varepsilon n\end{subarray}}\prescript{\mu}{r}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\underline{\smash{n}}}.

Such a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded Lie algebra
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mu}_{*}
is called an
\varepsilon
-spiral
of
\mathfrak{g}
. We also define a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded Lie subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}

\displaystyle\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{l}}^{\mu}_{*}=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{l}}^{\mu}_{n},\quad\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{l}}^{\mu}_{n}=\prescript{\mu}{\varepsilon n}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\underline{\smash{n}}}.

Such a Lie subalgebra
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{l}}^{\mu}_{*}
is called a
splitting
of the spiral
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mu}_{*}
. We let
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{l}}^{\mu}
be the same Lie algebra as
{\rm Phys.\penalty\ Rev.\penalty\ E}{\varepsilon}\mathfrak{l}^{\mu}_{*}
which has the grading forgotten.

We define the
\mathbf{Z}
-graded Lie algebra
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{u}}^{\mu}_{*}
with

\displaystyle\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{u}}^{\mu}_{*}=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{u}}^{\mu}_{n},\quad\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{u}}^{\mu}_{n}=\bigoplus_{r>\varepsilon n}\prescript{\mu}{r}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\underline{\smash{n}}}

to be the
nilpotent radical
of the spiral
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mu}_{*}
. Then
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{u}}^{\mu}_{*}
forms a homogeneous ideal of the graded Lie algebra
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mu}_{*}
and that for each
n\in\mathbf{Z}
the subspace
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{l}}^{\mu}_{n}\subseteq\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mu}_{n}
is mapped isomorphically onto the quotient
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mu}_{n}/\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{u}}^{\mu}_{n}
. Moreover,
{\rm Phys.\penalty\ Rev.\penalty\ E}{\varepsilon}\mathfrak{l}^{\mu}
is the Lie algebra of a pseudo-Levi subgroup of
G
, denoted by
L^{\mu}
, see
[
19
, 2.2.5]
.

We will write
\mathfrak{p}^{\mu}_{*}
,
\mathfrak{l}^{\mu}_{*}
and
\mathfrak{u}^{\mu}_{*}
instead of
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mu}_{*}
,
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{l}}^{\mu}_{*}
and
\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{u}}^{\mu}_{*}
when
\varepsilon
is clear from the context.

3.4
Admissible systems

An
admissible system
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
, as defined in
[
18
]
, is a datum
\left(M,\mathfrak{m}_{*},\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C}\right)
, where
M\subset G
is a subgroup whose Lie algebra is equipped with a
\mathbf{Z}
-grading
\mathfrak{m}_{*}=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\mathfrak{m}_{n}
arising as splitting
\mathfrak{l}^{\mu}_{*}
of some spiral,
\mathrm{O}\subset\mathfrak{m}^{\mathrm{nil}}
is a
M
-orbit such that
\mathrm{O}\cap\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}\neq\emptyset
and
\mathscr{C}\in\Loc_{M}(\mathrm{O})
is a cuspidal local system in the sense of
[
15
]
. We denote
\mathrm{O}_{\eta}=\mathrm{O}\cap\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}
and
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}=\mathscr{C}\mid_{\mathrm{O}_{\eta}}
. It is known that
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
is an irreducible
M_{0}
-equivariant local system which is
clean
, see
[
16
, §4]
.

An isomorphism of admissible systems
(M,\mathfrak{m}_{*},\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})\cong(M^{\prime},\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{*},\mathrm{O}^{\prime},\mathscr{C}^{\prime})
is a pair
(g,\varphi)
of element
g\in{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}
such that
gMg^{-1}=M^{\prime}
,
\Ad_{g}\mathfrak{m}_{n}=\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{n}
,
\Ad_{g}\mathrm{O}=\mathrm{O}^{\prime}
and an isomorphism
\varphi:g^{*}\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\cong\mathscr{C}
. Let
\mathfrak{T}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)
denote the groupoid of admissible systems on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
and let
\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{T}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)
denote the set of isomorphism classes its objects.

3.5
Extra torus actions

We will consider two one-dimensional tori
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}=\mathbf{C}^{\times}
and
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}=\mathbf{C}^{\times}
. We let
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
act on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
by weight
-2
and we let it acts trivially on
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
. The torus
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
acts
§
§

§

As it is said in the beginning of
, the torus
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
acts morally by loop rotation. We will see in
why the
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-action is defined in this way.
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
by
(\eta-\lambda_{0})/m
and on
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}
by
-\lambda_{0}/m
. Then the product
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}\rtimes{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
acts on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
in a natural way.

Given any
\mu\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(G_{\underline{\smash{0}}})_{\mathbf{Q}}
, consider the
\varepsilon
-spiral
\mathfrak{p}^{\mu}_{*}
and the splitting
\mathfrak{l}^{\mu}_{*}
. We let
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
acts on
\mathfrak{p}^{\mu}_{*}
by weight
-2
and trivially on the groups
L^{\mu}
and
P^{\mu}_{0}
. For each
n\in\mathbf{Z}
, we let
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
act on
\mathfrak{p}_{n}
by
(n-\lambda_{0})/m
for each
n\in\mathbf{Z}
. This
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-action, when restricted to the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{l}^{\mu}_{*}
, can be integrated to an
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-action on the group
L
. The product
L\rtimes{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
acts on
\mathfrak{l}^{\mu}
(
\mathbf{Z}
-grading forgotten) in a natural way. Similarly, the group
L^{\mu}_{0}\rtimes{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
(resp.
P^{\mu}_{0}\rtimes{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
) acts on the
\mathbf{Z}
-graded Lie algebra
\mathfrak{l}^{\mu}_{*}
(resp.
\mathfrak{p}^{\mu}_{*}
).

We also want to consider the
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-equivariance of sheaves on Lie algebras. However, not every cuspidal local system admits an extra
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-equivariance. For this reason, let
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t^{1/2m}}}\to{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
be the
2m
-fold cover. Hereafter, for any group
H
on which
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
acts by automorphism, we denote
¶
¶

¶

The use of the index
\diamond
differs slightly from that in
. We are obliged to take a finite cover of
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
so as to make the cuspidal local system equivariant. This technical point was omitted there.

H_{\diamond}=H\rtimes{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t^{1/2m}}}
and for any group
K
, we denote
K_{q}=K\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
.

Proposition 3.6
.

Given any admissible system
(M,\mathfrak{m}_{*},\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
, the cuspidal local system
\mathscr{C}
can be enhanced to a
{M_{\diamond,q}}
-equivariant local system on
\mathrm{O}
in a unique way.

Démonstration.

Pick
e\in\mathrm{O}_{\eta}
. Since we have
\Loc_{M}(\mathrm{O})\cong\Rep\pi_{0}(Z_{M}(e))
, and
\Loc_{{M_{\diamond,q}}}(\mathrm{O})\cong\Rep\pi_{0}(Z_{{M_{\diamond,q}}}(e))
it suffices to show
subsection 3.5
below.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
\mu\in\mathbf{X}_{*}({G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}})
and
(L^{\mu},\mathfrak{l}^{\mu}_{*})
the corresponding
\mathbf{Z}
-graded pseudo-Levi. Let
e\in\mathfrak{l}^{\mu}_{\eta}
. Then the following inclusions

Z_{L^{\mu}}(e)\subseteq Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(e)\subseteq Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond,q}}(e)

induce isomorphisms on the component groups

\pi_{0}(Z_{L^{\mu}}(e))\cong\pi_{0}(Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(e))\cong\pi_{0}(Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond,q}}(e)).

Démonstration.

Complete
e
into an
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple
\phi=(e,h,f)
with
h\in\mathfrak{l}^{\mu}_{0}
and
f\in\mathfrak{l}^{\mu}_{-\eta}
, which is possible by the
\mathbf{Z}
-graded Jacobson–Morosov theorem
[
16
, 3.3]
. Let
\varphi=\exp(h)\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(L_{0})
. Since the map

\displaystyle L^{\mu}\times\mathbf{C}^{\times}

\displaystyle\to L^{\mu}\rtimes{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t^{1/2m}}}

\displaystyle(g,\tau)

\displaystyle\mapsto(g\varphi(\tau^{-\eta})\lambda_{0}(\tau^{2}),\tau)

restricts to an isomorphism

\displaystyle Z_{L^{\mu}}(\phi)\times\mathbf{C}^{\times}\cong Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(\phi),

we obtain isomorphisms on their component groups.

\pi_{0}(Z_{L^{\mu}}(e))\cong\pi_{0}(Z_{L^{\mu}}(\phi))\cong\pi_{0}(Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(\phi))\cong\pi_{0}(Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(e)),

where the first (resp. the last) isomorphism is due to the fact that
Z_{L^{\mu}}(\phi)\subset Z_{L^{\mu}}(e)
(resp.
Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(\phi)\subset Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(e)
) is a maximal reductive subgroup, see
[
13
, 2.1]
.

For the
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}}
-equivariance, we make use of the group
Z^{q}_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(\phi)
introduced in (
) and the isomorphism

Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(\phi)\times\mathbf{C}^{\times}\cong Z^{q}_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(\phi)

to show that

\pi_{0}(Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(e))\cong\pi_{0}(Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(\phi))\cong\pi_{0}(Z^{q}_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond}}(\phi))\cong\pi_{0}(Z_{L^{\mu}_{\diamond,q}}(e)).

∎

Remark 3.8
.

As explained in
section 2
, we think of
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
as the graded piece
\mathfrak{g}_{\aff,\eta}
of the loop group
\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}=\mathfrak{g}[\varpi^{\pm 1}]
. Under this identification, the action of
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
defined as above coincides with the loop rotation on
\mathfrak{g}_{\aff,\eta}
.

3.9
Spiral induction

With the datum
\left(\mathfrak{p}_{*},\mathfrak{l}_{*},\mathfrak{u}_{*}\right)=\left(\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mu}_{*},\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{l}}^{\mu}_{*},\prescript{\varepsilon}{}{\mathfrak{u}}^{\mu}_{*}\right)
of a spiral together with a splitting, we can define the functor of induction. Let
P_{0}=\exp(\mathfrak{p}_{0})
and
L_{0}=\exp(\mathfrak{l}_{0})
.

Fix a sign
\varepsilon\in\left\{1,-1\right\}
. We consider the following diagram

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\xleftarrow{\alpha}G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\times^{P_{0}}\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}\xleftarrow{\beta}\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathfrak{l}_{\eta},

where

\displaystyle\quad\alpha(g,x)=\Ad(g)x;\quad\beta(x)=(e,x);\quad\gamma(x)=x\mod{\mathfrak{u}_{\eta}}.

Then, the morphism
\alpha
is proper whereas
\gamma
is a trivial vector bundle. The above sequence gives rise to a sequence of quotient stacks :

\displaystyle\xleftarrow{a}[{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}\times^{P_{0}}\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}/{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}]\xleftarrow{b}[\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}/P_{0,\diamond,q}]\xrightarrow{c}[\mathfrak{l}_{\eta}/L_{0,\diamond,q}],

where
b
is an isomorphism and
a
is proper. The spiral induction is defined as

(3.10)

\displaystyle\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}}

\displaystyle=(ab)_{*}c^{*}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{L_{0,\diamond,q}}\left(\mathfrak{l}_{\eta}\right)\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right).

In fact, for the sign
\varepsilon=\eta/|\eta|
, it induce functors on the nilpotent cones
[
18
, 7.1(a)]
:

\displaystyle\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{L_{0,\diamond,q}}\left(\mathfrak{l}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\eta}\right)\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right).

The spiral induction functor satisfies the following transitivity property with the parabolic induction :

(3.11)

\displaystyle\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}}=\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}_{\eta}}\circ\Ind^{\mathfrak{h}_{\eta}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\eta}}

whenever there is a spiral
\mathfrak{q}_{*}
with splitting
\mathfrak{h}_{*}
such that
\mathfrak{p}_{*}\subset\mathfrak{q}_{*}
and
\mathfrak{l}_{*}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{*}
. Here
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{*}=\mathfrak{p}_{*}\cap\mathfrak{h}_{*}
is a parabolic subalgebra of
\mathfrak{h}_{*}
.

3.12
Block decomposition

Suppose that
\varepsilon=\eta/|\eta|
. Let
\xi=(M,\mathfrak{m}_{*},\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})\in\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{T}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)
be an admissible system on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
. By the cleanness of cuspidal local systems
[
16
, §4]
, the
*
-extension and the
!
-extension of
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
are isomorphic to (a shift of) the intersection complex
\IC(\mathscr{C}_{\eta})
. Therefore, we may identify
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
with its direct image on
\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}
.

Define
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}^{\mathrm{nil}}\right)_{\xi}
to be the thick triangulated subcategory of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}^{\mathrm{nil}}\right)
generated by the constituents of the perverse cohomology of
\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{\eta}}\mathscr{C}
for all
\varepsilon
-spiral
\mathfrak{p}_{*}
which has
\mathfrak{m}_{*}
as splitting. We define
\mathrm{Perv}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}^{\mathrm{nil}}\right)_{\xi}
to be the intersection of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}^{\mathrm{nil}}\right)_{\xi}
with
\mathrm{Perv}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}^{\mathrm{nil}}\right)
. We call these subcategories and subsets the
blocks
of
\xi
.

According to
[
18
, 0.6]
, there are orthogonal decompositions

\displaystyle\mathrm{Perv}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)=\bigoplus_{\xi\in\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{T}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)}\mathrm{Perv}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)_{\xi},\qquad\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)=\bigoplus_{\xi\in\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{T}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)}\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)_{\xi}.

Using the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence, we can deduce the same decompositions with the extra torus actions :

(3.13)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Perv}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)=\bigoplus_{\xi\in\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{T}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)}\mathrm{Perv}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)_{\xi},\qquad\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)=\bigoplus_{\xi\in\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{T}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)}\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{nil}}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}\right)_{\xi}.

4
Relative affine root system and affine Weyl group

This section is a reminder of the affine alcove complex defined in
[
19
]
and its relations with spirals and splittings. We retain the setting of
section 3
.

4.1
Affine root hyperplane arrangement

From now on, we fix a maximal torus
T\subset G^{\lambda_{0}}
so that
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(T)
. Let
{\mathbb{A}}_{\diamond}
denote the vector space
\mathbf{X}_{*}(T_{\diamond})_{\mathbf{Q}}=\mathbf{X}_{*}(T_{\diamond})\otimes\mathbf{Q}
. Since

\mathbf{X}_{*}(T_{\diamond})=\mathbf{X}_{*}(T)\times\mathbf{X}_{*}({\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t^{1/2m}}})=\mathbf{X}_{*}(T)\times 2m\mathbf{X}_{*}({\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}),

we let
(0,2m)\in{\mathbb{A}}_{\diamond}
denote the inclusion of
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t^{1/2m}}}
in
T_{\diamond}
and let
\delta=(0,1)\in{\mathbb{A}}_{\diamond}^{*}
denote the character given by the obvious map
T_{\diamond}\to{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
so that
\delta(\mu,1)=1
for all
\mu\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(T)
. Notice that the defining character of
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t^{1/2m}}}
is
(1/2m)\delta
. Let
{\mathbb{A}}=\delta^{-1}(1)\subset{\mathbb{A}}_{\diamond}
, which is an affine subspace whose tangent space is
\mathbf{X}_{*}(T)_{\mathbf{Q}}
.

The root system of
G
is a subset
R(G,T)\subset\mathbf{X}^{*}(T)
. We define
R_{\aff}=R(G,T)\oplus\mathbf{Z}\delta\subset{\mathbb{A}}^{*}_{\diamond}
to be the set of (real) affine roots.

Each affine root
\alpha\in R_{\aff}
restricts to a non-constant affine function on
{\mathbb{A}}
, whose zero locus is a hyperplane in
{\mathbb{A}}
, denoted by
H_{\alpha}
and called
root hyperplane
. Let
\mathfrak{H}=\left\{H_{\alpha}\;;\;\alpha\in R_{\aff}\right\}
be the collection of root hyperplanes. These affine hyperplanes yield a stratification of
{\mathbb{A}}
into
facets
(i.e. subsets of
{\mathbb{A}}
determined by a finite number of equations
\alpha=0
or
\alpha>0
with
\alpha\in R_{\aff}
). Let
\mathfrak{F}
denote the collection of facets. The facets of maximal dimension are called
alcoves
. The set of alcoves is denoted by
\mathfrak{A}
.

The Killing form on
\mathfrak{g}
yields a Euclidean space structure on
{\mathbb{A}}
. The
affine Weyl group
(with respect to
T
) is the group
W=N_{G}(T)/T\ltimes\mathbf{X}_{*}(T)
, which acts on
{\mathbb{A}}
by orthogonal reflections and translations. This action preserves the set
\mathfrak{H}
of root hyperplanes, inducing thus a
W
-action on
\mathfrak{F}
, whose restriction to
\mathfrak{A}\subseteq\mathfrak{F}
is simply transitive. Since
{\mathbb{A}}_{\diamond}
is the linearisation of
{\mathbb{A}}
, the affine
W
-action on
{\mathbb{A}}
extends in a unique way to a linear
W
-action on
{\mathbb{A}}_{\diamond}
.

Let
\mathfrak{E}
be the collection of affine subspaces of
{\mathbb{A}}
which are non-empty intersection of a finite subset of
\mathfrak{H}
. Elements of
\mathfrak{E}
are called
relevant subspaces
.

4.2
Canonical affine Weyl group

Given an alcove
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
, let
\Delta^{\kappa}\subseteq R_{\aff}
be the subset of affine simple roots
\alpha
such that
\alpha>0
on
\kappa
and
H_{\alpha}\cap\overline{\kappa}
is a face of the simplex
\overline{\kappa}
. It is known that
\Delta^{\kappa}
forms a base for the affine root system
({\mathbb{A}},R_{\aff})
. For each
\alpha\in R_{\aff}
, let
s_{\alpha}\in W
denote the orthogonal reflection on
{\mathbb{A}}
with respect to the root hyperplane
H_{\alpha}
. Then injective map
\Delta^{\kappa}\hookrightarrow W
defined by
\alpha\mapsto s_{\alpha}
yields a Coxeter group
\left(W,\Delta^{\kappa}\right)
.

Since
W
acts simply transitively on
\mathfrak{A}
, given any two alcoves
\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{A}
, there is a unique
w\in W
such that
\kappa^{\prime}=w\kappa
. Then,
w
yields an isomorphism of Coxeter groups

\displaystyle(W,\Delta^{\kappa})

\displaystyle\cong(W,\Delta^{\kappa^{\prime}})

\displaystyle y\mapsto wyw^{-1},

\displaystyle s_{\alpha}\mapsto ws_{\alpha}w^{-1}=s_{w\alpha}.

Thus we can define

\left(\mathcal{W},\Delta\right)=\varprojlim_{\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}}\left(W,\Delta^{\kappa}\right)

to be the
canonical affine Weyl group
: for any
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
, there is a canonical isomorphism
\left(\mathcal{W},\Delta\right)\cong\left(W,\Delta^{\kappa}\right)
.

For any subset
J\subseteq\Delta
, we denote by
\left(\mathcal{W}_{J},J\right)
the Coxeter sub-system of
\left(\mathcal{W},\Delta\right)
generated by
J
. For any alcove
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
, we denote by
\left(W_{J^{\kappa}},J^{\kappa}\right)
the Coxeter subgroup of
\left(W,\Delta^{\kappa}\right)
which is the image of
\left(\mathcal{W}_{J},J\right)
in
(W,\Delta^{\kappa})
under the canonical isomorphism
\left(\mathcal{W},\Delta\right)\cong\left(W,\Delta^{\kappa}\right)
.

We define a
\mathcal{W}
-action on
\mathfrak{A}
as follows : for
w\in\mathcal{W}
and
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
, put
w\kappa=(w^{\kappa})^{-1}\kappa
. This action is simply transitive and commutes with the
W
-action on
\mathfrak{A}
.

Remark 4.3
.

It is useful to keep in mind that
\mathcal{W}=\Aut_{W}(\mathfrak{A})
and that the
\mathcal{W}
-action and the
W
-action on
\mathfrak{A}
are of different nature. The
W
-action on
\mathfrak{A}
is induced from a reflection action on
{\mathbb{A}}
, whereas the
\mathcal{W}
-action on
\mathfrak{A}
does not extend continuously to an action on
{\mathbb{A}}
. Moreover, the
\mathcal{W}
-action is “local” in the sense that whenever
s\in\mathcal{W}
is a simple reflection,
s\kappa
and
\kappa
are adjacent for every alcove
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
.

4.4
Facets and Coxeter subgroups

We put a partial order
\leq
on the set of facets
\mathfrak{F}
by the inclusion of closure :
\sigma\leq\tau
if
\sigma\subseteq\overline{\tau}
. Given any
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
and
J\subsetneq\Delta
, we define a facet
\partial_{J}\kappa\in\mathfrak{F}
with
\partial_{J}\kappa\leq\kappa
by

\partial_{J}\kappa=\left\{y\in{\mathbb{A}}\;;\;\begin{aligned} &\alpha(y)=0,&\forall\alpha\in J^{\kappa}\\
&\alpha(y)>0,&\forall\alpha\in\Delta^{\kappa}\setminus J^{\kappa}\end{aligned}\right\}.

Then
W_{J^{\kappa}}
coincides with the pointwise stabiliser
W_{\partial_{J}\kappa}=\Stab_{W}(\partial_{J}\kappa)
.

Given any facet
\sigma\in\mathfrak{F}
, choose an alcove
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
such that
\sigma\leq\kappa
. There is a unique subset
J\subsetneq\Delta
such that
\partial_{J}\kappa=\sigma
and moreover, the subset
J
is independent of the choice of
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
. The subset
J\subsetneq\Delta
is called the
type
of the facet
\sigma\in\mathfrak{F}
. Let
\mathfrak{F}_{J}=\partial_{J}(\mathfrak{A})\subseteq\mathfrak{F}
denote the set of facets of type
J
. It follows that
\mathfrak{F}=\bigsqcup_{J\subsetneq\Delta}\mathfrak{F}_{J}
and
\mathfrak{F}_{\emptyset}=\mathfrak{A}
. Moreover, each subset
\mathfrak{F}_{J}\subset\mathfrak{F}
is stable by the action of
W
and the map
\partial_{J}:\mathfrak{A}\to\mathfrak{F}_{J}
induces a bijection of
W
-sets
\mathfrak{A}/\mathcal{W}_{J}\cong\mathfrak{F}_{J}
.

For any pair of strict subsets
J\subseteq K\subsetneq\Delta
, the boundary map
\partial_{K}:\mathfrak{A}\to\mathfrak{F}_{K}
descends to a map
\partial^{J}_{K}:\mathfrak{F}_{J}\to\mathfrak{F}_{K}
such that
\partial_{K}=\partial^{J}_{K}\circ\partial_{J}
. By abuse of notation, we will write
\partial_{K}=\partial^{J}_{K}
.

4.5
Correspondence between spirals and facets

We define
\mathbf{x}=(\lambda_{0}/m,1)\in{\mathbb{A}}
. Let
\mathfrak{P}_{T}
be the set of spirals
\mathfrak{p}_{*}
such that
\mathfrak{p}_{*}={\rm Phys.\penalty\ Rev.\penalty\ E}{\varepsilon}\mathfrak{p}_{*}^{\mu}
for some
\mu\in\mathbf{X}_{*}\left(T\right)_{\mathbf{Q}}
,
cf.

subsection 3.3
.

Given a facet
\sigma\in\mathfrak{F}
, we choose a point
y\in\sigma
. Since both
y
and
\mathbf{x}
lie in
{\mathbb{A}}
, the difference
\mathbf{x}-y
is in
\mathbf{X}_{*}(T)_{\mathbf{Q}}
. Set
\mu_{y}=m\varepsilon\left(\mathbf{x}-y\right)\in\mathbf{X}_{*}\left(T\right)_{\mathbf{Q}}
. It gives rise to the spiral
{\rm Phys.\penalty\ Rev.\penalty\ E}{\varepsilon}\mathfrak{p}^{\mu_{y}}_{*}
, which does not depend on the choice of
y\in\sigma
, see
[
19
, 3.4.4]
.

We denote
\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{*}={\rm Phys.\penalty\ Rev.\penalty\ E}{\varepsilon}\mathfrak{p}^{\mu_{y}}_{*}
as well as
\mathfrak{u}^{\sigma}_{*}={\rm Phys.\penalty\ Rev.\penalty\ E}{\varepsilon}\mathfrak{u}^{\mu_{y}}_{*}
and
\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{*}={\rm Phys.\penalty\ Rev.\penalty\ E}{\varepsilon}\mathfrak{l}^{\mu_{y}}_{*}
for any choice of
y\in\sigma
. We also denote
P^{\sigma}_{0}=\exp\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{0}\right)
,
L^{\sigma}=\exp\left(\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}\right)
,
L^{\sigma}_{0}=\exp\left(\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{0}\right)
and
U^{\sigma}_{0}=\exp\left(\mathfrak{u}^{\sigma}_{0}\right)
; those are subgroups of
G
.

Recall that we have defined in
subsection 3.5
a
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
-action on
\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{*}
. The
T_{\diamond}
-weights appearing in
\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{*}
can be characterised as follows :

Proposition 4.6
.

An affine root
\alpha\in R_{\aff}
appears in
\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{*}
if and only if for some (equiv. every)
y\in\sigma
, we have

\alpha(y)\leq 0
in the case
\varepsilon=1
;

\alpha(y)\geq 0
in the case
\varepsilon=-1
.

In this case, the
\alpha
-weight space appears in
\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{n}
for
n=m\,\alpha(\mathbf{x})\in\mathbf{Z}
. ∎

This proposition yields a combinatorial parametrisation of the set of
T
-stable
\varepsilon
-spirals.

Corollary 4.7
.

The assignment
\sigma\mapsto\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{*}
yields a bijection between
\mathfrak{F}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{T}
. Moreover,
\sigma\leq\tau
holds if and only if
\mathfrak{p}^{\tau}_{*}\subseteq\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{*}
. ∎

Remark 4.8
.

In other words,
\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{*}
recovers the usual notion of parahoric subalgebra of the loop group
G(\!(\varpi^{-\varepsilon})\!)
and
\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{n}
is the
(n/m)
-weight space for the fractional cocharacter
\mathbf{x}=(\lambda_{0}/m,1)\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(T_{\diamond})_{\mathbf{Q}}
. The space
{\mathbb{A}}
with the facet structure
\mathfrak{F}
is the apartment of the Bruhat–Tits building for
G(\!(\varpi^{-\varepsilon})\!)
given by the parahoric subgroups containing
T
and stabilised by the loop rotation
{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t}}
.

4.9
Pseudo-Levi attached to relevant affine subspaces

Let
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathbf{Z}-\gr}_{T}
be the set of quadruples
(M,M_{0},\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m}_{*})
with
M
a pseudo-Levi subgroup of
G
containing the maximal torus
T
and
\mathfrak{m}_{*}
a
\mathbf{Z}
-grading on
\mathfrak{m}=\Lie M
which makes
\mathfrak{m}
a graded Lie algebra such that
T\subseteq M_{0}
, where
M_{0}=\exp\left(\mathfrak{m}_{0}\right)
. There is bijective correspondence

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}\longleftrightarrow\mathfrak{M}^{\mathbf{Z}-\gr}_{T}

defined as follows : given any relevant subspace
\mathbb{E}\in\mathfrak{E}
, choosing any facet
\sigma\in\mathfrak{F}
which spans
\mathbb{E}
as affine subspace, we set

\displaystyle\left(M^{\mathbb{E}},M^{\mathbb{E}}_{0},\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}},\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}}_{*}\right)=\left(L^{\sigma},L^{\sigma}_{0},\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma},\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{*}\right);

then the splitting
\left(M^{\mathbb{E}},M^{\mathbb{E}}_{0},\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}},\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}}_{*}\right)
does not depend on the choice of
\sigma
, see
[
19
, 3.4.7]
. Similarly to the case of spiral, we can characterise the
T_{\diamond}
-weights appearing in
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}}_{*}
as follows :

Proposition 4.10
.

An affine root
\alpha\in R_{\aff}
appears in
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}}_{*}
if and only if
\alpha\mid_{\mathbb{E}}=0
. In this case, the
\alpha
-weight space appears in
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}}_{n}
for
n=m\,\alpha(\mathbf{x})
. ∎

We can describe the root system of the reductive group
M^{\mathbb{E}}_{\diamond}=M^{\mathbb{E}}\rtimes{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{t^{1/2m}}}
with respect to the maximal torus
T_{\diamond}
as a subsystem of the affine root system
({\mathbb{A}},R_{\aff})
:

Proposition 4.11
.

Given a relevant subspace
\mathbb{E}\in\mathfrak{E}
, we put
R_{\mathbb{E}}=\{\alpha\in R_{\aff}\;;\;\alpha\mid_{\mathbb{E}}=0\}
. Denote
(M,\mathfrak{m}_{*})=(M^{\mathbb{E}},\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}}_{*})
. Then the following statements hold :

(i)

R_{\mathbb{E}}
coincides with the root system
R(M_{\diamond},T_{\diamond})
as subset of
\mathbf{X}^{*}(T_{\diamond})_{\mathbf{Q}}
.

(ii)

The vector subspace
\mathbb{E}_{\diamond}\subset{\mathbb{A}}_{\diamond}
spanned by
\mathbb{E}
coincides with
\mathbf{X}_{*}(Z(M_{\diamond}))_{\mathbf{Q}}
.

(iii)

The pointwise stabiliser
W_{\mathbb{E}}=\Stab_{W}(\mathbb{E})
coincides with the Weyl group
W_{M_{\diamond}}=W(M_{\diamond},T_{\diamond})
.

(iv)

The stabiliser
W_{\mathbb{E},\mathbf{x}}=\Stab_{W_{\mathbb{E}}}(\mathbf{x})
coincides with the Weyl group
W_{M_{0,\diamond}}=W(M_{0,\diamond},T_{\diamond})
.

Démonstration.

The statement
(i)
follows immediately from
subsection 4.9
. The statement
(ii)
follows from
(i)
by taking the intersection of the kernels of elements of
R_{\mathbb{E}}=R(M_{\diamond},T_{\diamond})
.

Via the reflection action of
W_{M_{\diamond}}
on
\mathbf{X}_{*}\left(T_{\diamond}\right)
, we may consider
W_{M_{\diamond}}
as subgroup of
W
. Let
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
be an alcove such that
\overline{\kappa}\cap\mathbb{E}\neq\emptyset
. Then there is a subset
J^{\kappa}\subset\Delta^{\kappa}
such that
\mathbb{E}=\left\{y\in{\mathbb{A}}\;;\;\alpha(y)=0,\;\forall\alpha\in J^{\kappa}\right\}
. Consequently,
J^{\kappa}
is a base for the root system
R_{\mathbb{E}}
and the subgroup
W_{\mathbb{E}}=W_{J^{\kappa}}
is its Weyl group. We conclude that
W_{\mathbb{E}}
coincides with
W(M_{\diamond},T_{\diamond})
, whence
(iii)
.

Now let
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}
be the smallest relevant affine subspace containing both
\mathbb{E}
and
\mathbf{x}
. It follows that
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}}_{0}=\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}}_{n}=0
for
n\neq 0
. Then
(iii)
applied to
(M^{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}},\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}}_{*})
yields
(iv)
.
∎

In particular, if we let
\mathbb{E}\in\mathfrak{E}
be the smallest relevant affine subspace containing
\mathbf{x}\in{\mathbb{A}}
, then by
subsection 4.9
we have
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}}_{0}=\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
and
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}}_{n}=0
for
n\neq 0
. Hence
M^{\mathbb{E}}_{0}=G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
and
subsection 4.9
(iv)
yields the following :

Corollary 4.12
.

The subset
R_{\mathbf{x}}=\left\{\alpha\in R_{\aff}\;;\;\alpha(\mathbf{x})=0\right\}
coincides with the root system
R({G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}},T_{\diamond})
and the stabiliser
W_{\mathbf{x}}=\Stab_{W}(\mathbf{x})
coincides with the Weyl group
W({G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}},T_{\diamond})
. Moreover, the inclusion of pairs
({G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}},T)\hookrightarrow({G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}},{T_{\diamond}})
induces an isomorphism
W({G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}},T)\cong W_{\mathbf{x}}
. ∎

4.13
Relative affine root system attached to an admissible system

Let
\xi=\left(M,\mathfrak{m}_{*},\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C}\right)
be an admissible system on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
as defined in
subsection 3.4
. In particular,
\mathscr{C}
is a
{M_{\diamond,q}}
-equivariant cuspidal local system on
\mathrm{O}\subset\mathfrak{m}
. There exists a relevant hyperplane
\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}^{M}
such that
(M,\mathfrak{m}_{*})=(M^{\mathbb{E}},\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}}_{*})
as in
subsection 4.9
. We assume that
\dim\mathbb{E}^{M}>0
.

The restriction of the affine root system
({\mathbb{A}},R_{\aff})
to the subspace
\mathbb{E}^{M}
yields an affine root system
(\mathbb{E}^{M},R^{\prime}_{\xi})
, where

R^{\prime}_{\xi}=\left\{\alpha\mid_{\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond}}\;;\;\alpha\in R_{\aff}\;;\;a\mid_{\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond}}\neq 0\right\}
. The root system
(\mathbb{E}^{M},R^{\prime}_{\xi})
may not be reduced. Let
R_{\xi}\subset R^{\prime}_{\xi}
be the subset of indivisible roots.

We let
\Xi\subset\mathfrak{F}
denote the set of facets which span
\mathbb{E}^{M}
as affine subspace of
{\mathbb{A}}
. The elements of
\Xi
are called
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-alcoves
. Notice that by
subsection 4.9
, an
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-alcove
\nu\in\Xi
is characterised by the property that
\mathfrak{l}^{\nu}_{*}=\mathfrak{m}_{*}
. Let
W_{M}=W(M_{\diamond},T_{\diamond})\subset W
be the Weyl group of
M_{\diamond}
. By
subsection 4.9
, it is also the pointwise stabiliser of
\mathbb{E}^{M}
. As we have explained in
subsection 2.7
, the set
\Xi
will be used to parametrise the fixed components of the partial affine Springer resolution
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}
.

The main properties, proven in
[
19
, 2.4]
, are the following :

Proposition 4.14
.

The following statements hold :

(i)

R_{\xi}
is an irreducible affine root system on
\mathbb{E}^{M}
. Let
W_{\xi}
denote its Weyl group.

(ii)

W_{\xi}
is isomorphic to
N_{W}(W_{M})/W_{M}
.

(iii)

Given any
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-facet
\nu\in\Xi
, there is a unique base
\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi}\subset R_{\xi}
such that the elements of
\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi}
take positive values on
\nu
.

(iv)

W_{\xi}
acts simply transitively on
\Xi
. Consequently, all the
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-alcoves are of the same type.

∎

By
subsection 4.13

(iv)
, we can denote the type of
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-facets by
I_{\xi}\subset\Delta
, so that
\Xi\subset\mathfrak{F}_{I_{\xi}}
.

4.15
Symmetry of
\xi

We keep the admissible system
\xi
as in
subsection 4.13
and the type
I_{\xi}\subset\Delta
. Let
\mathbf{x}^{M}\in\mathbb{E}^{M}
denote the image of
\mathbf{x}
under the orthogonal projection
{\mathbb{A}}\to\mathbb{E}^{M}
. We denote
W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}=\Stab_{W_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x}^{M})
.

Proposition 4.16
.

The following statements hold :

(i)

The action of
N_{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}}(Z_{M_{\diamond}})
on
Z_{M_{\diamond}}
by conjugation yields an isomorphism
N_{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}}(Z_{M_{\diamond}})/M_{0,\diamond}\cong W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}
.

(ii)

The adjoint action of
N_{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}}(Z_{M_{\diamond}})
on
\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}
preserves the
M_{0,\diamond}
-orbit
\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\subset\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}
.

(iii)

The cuspidal local system
\mathscr{C}
has a
N_{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}}(Z_{M_{\diamond}})
-equivariant structure.

Démonstration.

Let
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{E}
be the smallest relevant affine subspace which contains
\mathbf{x}^{M}
. It follows that
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{E}
and
(M^{\prime},\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{*}):=(M^{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}},\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}}_{*})
defined in
subsection 4.9
is a
\mathbf{Z}
-graded pseudo-Levi subgroup of
G
which contains
(M,\mathfrak{m}_{*})
as graded Levi subgroup. We may view
\xi
as an admissible system on
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{\diamond}
in the sense of
[
15
]
. We have the isomorphisms of groups below.

(i)

By
subsection 4.9
, the Weyl group
W({G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}},{T_{\diamond}})
coincides with
W_{\mathbf{x}}
.

(ii)

We can deduce from it that
N_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}}}(Z_{{M_{\diamond}}})/{M_{0,\diamond}}
is isomorphic to
W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}=\Stab_{W_{\xi}}(\mathbf{x}^{M})
.

(iii)

W_{\sigma,\xi}=N_{M^{\prime}_{\diamond}}(Z_{M_{\diamond}})/M_{\diamond}
coincides with the pointwise stabiliser of
\sigma
in
W_{\xi}
;

(iv)

W_{\sigma,\xi,\mathbf{x}}=N_{M^{\prime}_{0,\diamond}}(Z_{M_{\diamond}})/M_{0,\diamond}
coincides with the stabiliser of
\mathbf{x}^{M}\in\mathbb{E}^{M}
in
W_{\sigma,\xi}
.

By the choice of
\sigma
, we have
W_{\sigma,\xi,\mathbf{x}}=W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}
. Thus we deduce
N_{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}}(Z_{M_{\diamond}})=N_{M^{\prime}_{0,\diamond}}(Z_{M_{\diamond}})
.

Since
({M_{\diamond}},\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
is an admissible system on
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{\diamond}
, by
[
15
, 9.2]
the action of
N_{M^{\prime}_{\diamond}}(Z_{{M_{\diamond}}})
by conjugation on
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{\diamond}
preserves
\mathrm{O}\subset\mathfrak{m}_{\diamond}
and the cuspidal local system
\mathscr{C}
has a
N_{M^{\prime}_{\diamond}}(Z_{{M_{\diamond}}})
-equivariant structure. The restriction of this action to the subgroup
N_{M^{\prime}_{0,\diamond}}(Z_{{M_{\diamond}}})
preserves
\mathrm{O}_{\eta}
and induces an action on
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
. Since
N_{M^{\prime}_{0,\diamond}}(Z_{{M_{\diamond}}})=N_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond}}}(Z_{{M_{\diamond}}})
, the results follow.
∎

4.17
Canonical relative affine Weyl group

By
subsection 4.13
, the considerations of
subsection 4.2
and
subsection 4.4
can be applied to
\mathbb{E}^{M}
with the alcove structure
\mathfrak{F}(\mathbb{E}^{M})=\left\{\sigma\in\mathfrak{F}\;;\;\sigma\subset\mathbb{E}^{M}\right\}
:

—

Each
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-alcove
\nu\in\Xi
gives rise to a base
\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi}\subset R_{\xi}
and for any pair
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
, the
W_{\xi}
-action gives a canonical isomorphism of based affine root systems
∥
∥

∥

We omit the set of roots
R_{\xi}
in the datum
(\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond},\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi})
because it is determined by the Euclidean structure on
\mathbb{E}_{\xi}
and the base
\Delta^{\nu}\subset\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}^{*}
.

(\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond},\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi})\cong(\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond},\Delta^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\xi})
. We define the
canonical based relative affine root system
to be
(\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond},\Delta_{\xi})=\varprojlim_{\nu\in\Xi}(\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond},\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi})
. Set
\mathbb{E}_{\xi}=\delta^{-1}(1)\subset\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}
, which is a Euclidean affine space.

—

We define the
canonical relative affine Weyl group

(\mathcal{W}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})
to be the Weyl group of
(\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond},\Delta_{\xi})
. It acts by orthogonal reflections on
\mathbb{E}_{\xi}
and this action extends linearly to
\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}
. For each
\nu\in\Xi
, there is a canonical isomorphism
(\mathcal{W}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})\cong(W_{\xi},\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi})
.

—

The
\mathcal{W}
-action on
\mathfrak{A}
induces a
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
-action on
\mathfrak{F}_{I_{\xi}}\cong\mathfrak{A}/\mathcal{W}_{I_{\xi}}
. The latter action restricts to a simply transitive
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
-action on the subset
\Xi\subset\mathfrak{F}_{I_{\xi}}
which commutes with the
W_{\xi}
-action.

—

For each subset
J\subsetneq\Delta_{\xi}
, there is a boundary map
\partial_{J}:\Xi\to\mathfrak{F}(\mathbb{E}^{M})
of
relative type

J
. Put
\Xi_{J}=\partial_{J}(\Xi)
(in particular,
\Xi=\Xi_{\emptyset}
). There is a decomposition
\mathfrak{F}(\mathbb{E}^{M})=\bigsqcup_{J\subsetneq\Delta_{\xi}}\Xi_{J}
.

—

For each subset
J\subsetneq\Delta_{\xi}
, the map
\partial_{J}
induces a bijection of
W_{\xi}
-sets
\Xi/\mathcal{W}_{J}\cong\Xi_{J}
.

Proposition 4.18
.

Suppose that
\dim\mathbb{E}^{M}>0
. Write
I=I_{\xi}
. The following statements hold :

(i)

\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
is isomorphic to
N_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathcal{W}_{I})/\mathcal{W}_{I}
.

(ii)

The short exact sequence

1\to\mathcal{W}_{I}\to N_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathcal{W}_{I})\to\mathcal{W}_{\xi}\to 1

has a canonical splitting which takes any element
w\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
to the (unique) shortest element of the pre-image of
w
in
N_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathcal{W}_{I})
. We may thus regard
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
as a subgroup of
\mathcal{W}
.

(iii)

Let
\ell:\mathcal{W}\to\mathbf{N}
be the length function of the Coxeter group
(\mathcal{W},\Delta)
and let
\ell_{\xi}:\mathcal{W}_{\xi}\to\mathbf{N}
be that of
(\mathcal{W}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})
. If we regard
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
as a subgroup of
\mathcal{W}
, then for
w,w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
we have

\ell(ww^{\prime})=\ell(w)+\ell(w^{\prime})\Longleftrightarrow\ell_{\xi}(ww^{\prime})=\ell_{\xi}(w)+\ell_{\xi}(w^{\prime}).

Démonstration.

These statements are proven in
[
12
, 5.9]
for finite root systems. However, the same arguments work for affine root systems and Coxeter groups in general, provided that the parabolic subgroup
\mathcal{W}_{I}
is finite and satisfies the condition of
[
12
, 5.7.1]
.
∎

Remark 4.19
.

A complete list of the types
I_{\xi}\subset\Delta
with the relative affine root systems
(\mathbb{E}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})
is given in
[
11
, §6–§7]
for
({\mathbb{A}},\Delta)
untwisted and
[
10
, §11]
for
({\mathbb{A}},\Delta)
twisted, where the subset
I_{\xi}
is indicated as boxed vertices in the Dynkin diagram of
({\mathbb{A}},\Delta)
and the relative affine root system
(\mathbb{E}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})
is indicated in the
\flat-\sharp
-diagrams.

5
Construction of
\Phi

We keep the assumptions of
section 3
and
section 4
. In particular, there is an admissible system
\xi=\left(M,\mathfrak{m}_{*},\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C}\right)
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}

cf.

subsection 4.13
, a cocharacter
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbf{X}_{*}\left(T\right)
which lifts the
\mathbf{Z}/m
-grading on
\mathfrak{g}
, a sign
\varepsilon\in\left\{1,-1\right\}
and
\mathbf{x}=(\lambda_{0}/m,1)\in{\mathbb{A}}
.

Following the strategy of
[
25
]
and
[
19
]
, we will construct a homomorphism
\Phi:\mathbf{H}_{\xi}\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
from the degenerate double affine Hecke algebra (dDAHA) to the convolution algebra.

5.0
Notation for parabolic and spiral inductions

In
subsection 4.5
, we have introduced a bijection between
\mathfrak{F}
and the set of spirals
\mathfrak{p}_{*}
such that
T\subset P_{0}
. We have attached to each facet
\sigma\in\mathfrak{F}
a
\varepsilon
-spiral
\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{*}
and a splitting
\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{*}
. In the rest of the article, we will abbreviate the spiral induction (
subsection 3.9
) by

\displaystyle\Ind_{\sigma}=\Ind^{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{\eta}}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{L^{\sigma}_{0,\diamond,q}}(\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{\eta})\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}).

By
subsection 4.9

(i)
, the root system
R(L^{\sigma}_{\diamond},T_{\diamond})
can be identified with the root subsystem of
\alpha\in R_{\aff}
such that
\alpha\mid_{\sigma}=0
. Given another facet
\tau\in\mathfrak{F}
such that
\sigma\leq\tau
(
subsection 4.4
), we have
\mathfrak{p}^{\tau}_{*}\subseteq\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{*}
by
subsection 4.5
. Set
\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma\leq\tau}_{*}=\mathfrak{p}^{\tau}_{*}\cap\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{*}
, so that
\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma\leq\tau}_{*}
is a parabolic subalgebra of
\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{*}
. If we pick any point
y\in\tau
, then by
subsection 4.5
, the
T_{\diamond}
-weights of
\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma\leq\tau}_{*}
are those roots
\alpha\in R(L^{\sigma}_{\diamond},T_{\diamond})
such that
\varepsilon\cdot\alpha(y)\leq 0
. We will abbreviate the
\mathbf{Z}
-graded parabolic induction (
subsection 1.8
) by

\displaystyle\Ind^{\sigma}_{\tau}=\Ind^{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma\leq\tau}_{\eta}}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{L^{\tau}_{0,\diamond,q}}(\mathfrak{l}^{\tau}_{\eta})\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{L^{\sigma}_{0,\diamond,q}}(\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{\eta}).

5.1
Degenerate double affine Hecke algebra

Recall the canonical relative based root system
(\mathbb{E}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})
defined in
subsection 4.17
. We regard
\Delta_{\xi}
as linear functions on the linearisation
\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}
. The relative affine Weyl group
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
acts on
\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}
by orthogonal reflections. It induces a
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
-action on the algebra
\mathbf{S}_{\xi}
in which
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
acts trivially on
u
.

For each relative affine simple root
\alpha\in\Delta_{\xi}
, we define as in
[
19
]
an integer
c_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 2}
. Pick any
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-alcove
\nu\in\Xi
. The canonical isomorphism
(\mathbb{E}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})\cong(\mathbb{E}^{M},\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi})
sends
\alpha
to a relative root
\alpha^{\nu}\in\Delta^{\nu}_{\xi}
, which cuts out a relative hyperplane
H\subset\mathbb{E}^{M}
. Then
H\subset{\mathbb{A}}
, being a relevant subspace, gives rise to a pseudo-Levi subalgebra
\mathfrak{l}
which contains
\mathfrak{m}
as Levi subalgebra. The connected component of
\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\mathbf{R}}\setminus H_{\mathbf{R}}
on which
\alpha^{\nu}
takes positive values yields a parabolic subalgebra
\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathfrak{m}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{+}\subset\mathfrak{l}
. Pick any
e\in\mathrm{O}
. Then
c_{\alpha}
is defined to be the biggest integer
c\geq 2
such that
(\ad_{e})^{c-2}\neq 0
on
\mathfrak{u}^{+}
.

We define the
degenerate double affine Hecke algebra
(dDAHA)
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
attached to the admissible system
\xi
with the based relative affine root system
(\mathbb{E}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi})
. It is the associative algebra over the polynomial ring
\mathbf{k}[u]
generated by
\left\{x^{\mu}\right\}_{\mu\in\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}^{*}}
and
\left\{s_{\alpha}\right\}_{\alpha\in\Delta_{\xi}}
subject to the following relations for
\mu,\nu\in\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}^{*}
,
r\in\mathbf{Q}
and
\alpha\in\Delta_{\xi}
:

\displaystyle rx^{\mu}=x^{r\mu},\quad x^{\mu}+x^{\nu}=x^{\mu+\nu},\quad\mathbf{k}[s_{\alpha}\;;\;\alpha\in\Delta_{\xi}]\cong\mathbf{k}\mathcal{W}_{\xi}

\displaystyle s_{\alpha}x^{\mu}-x^{s_{\alpha}(\mu)}s_{\alpha}=uc_{\alpha}\langle\mu,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle.

The family
(c_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\Delta_{\xi}}
is usually called the
parameters
of
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
.

Let
\mathbf{k}[\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}]
be the algebra of
\mathbf{k}
-value polynomial functions on
\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}
. The subalgebra of
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
generated by the set
\left\{x^{\mu}\right\}_{\mu\in\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}^{*}}
is isomorphic to
\mathbf{k}[\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}]
in the obvious way. Therefore we may view
\mathbf{k}[\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}]
as a subalgebra of
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
and denote
\mu=x^{\mu}\in\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
for
\mu\in\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}^{*}
. The dDAHA
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
, as vector space, can be written as a tensor product

\mathbf{H}_{\xi}=\mathbf{S}_{\xi}\otimes\mathbf{k}\mathcal{W}_{\xi}

of two subalgebras : the polynomial subalgebra
\mathbf{S}_{\xi}=\mathbf{k}[\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond}]\otimes\mathbf{k}[u]
and the group ring of the canonical affine Weyl group
\mathbf{k}\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
. They satisfy the following commutation laws :

\displaystyle s_{\alpha}\;f-s_{\alpha}(f)\;s_{\alpha}=uc_{\alpha}\frac{f-s_{\alpha}(f)}{\alpha},\quad\alpha\in\Delta_{\xi},\quad f\in\mathbf{S}_{\xi}

The elements
u,\delta\in\mathbf{S}_{\xi}
are central in
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
.

Remark 5.2
.

The class of dDAHAs which can be constructed in the present setting is limited for non-simply laced root systems. Since the constants
c_{\alpha}
are certain integers determined by the cuspidal pair
(\mathrm{O},\mathscr{C})
, only certain integral proportions between parameters can appear. In the list of G. Lusztig
[
11
, §6–§7]

[
10
, §11]
, the number
c_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in\Delta_{\xi}
corresponds to the number
A
for in the vertex
\sharp^{A\times B}_{k}
or
\flat^{A\times B}_{k}
indicated in the
\flat-\sharp
-diagrams.

5.3
Extension algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}

We define the
Lusztig sheaf
for the
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-alcove
\nu\in\Xi
to be

\mathbf{I}^{\nu}=\Ind_{\nu}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}),

We define the set of
W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}
-conjugacy classes
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}=W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}\backslash\Xi
. Where
W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}
is as in
subsection 4.15
.
We show that
\mathbf{I}^{\nu}
depends only on the class
\underline{\smash{\nu}}
in
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}
. By
subsection 4.15
(iii)
, we may view the cuspidal local system
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
as
W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}
-equivariant local system over the stack
[\mathrm{O}_{\eta}/{M_{0,\diamond,q}}]
. If
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
are such that
\underline{\smash{\nu}}=\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}
(i.e. if they are in the same
W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}
-orbit), let
w\in W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}
be such that
w\nu=\nu^{\prime}
. Then
w
gives a stack automorphism
\Ad_{w}:[\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}/{M_{0,\diamond,q}}]\cong[\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}/{M_{0,\diamond,q}}]
. The
W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}
-equivariance of
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
gives an isomorphism
\Ad_{w}^{*}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}\cong\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
. Consequently, we have canonical isomorphisms

\mathbf{I}^{\nu^{\prime}}=\Ind_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}\cong\Ind_{\nu}\Ad_{w}^{*}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}\cong\Ind_{\nu}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}=\mathbf{I}^{\nu}.

Proposition 5.4
.

For
\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}
, the following statements hold

(i)

We have
\mathbf{I}^{\nu}\cong\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbf{Z}}\prescript{p}{}{\mathscr{H}}^{k}\mathbf{I}^{\nu}[-k]
and each factor
\prescript{p}{}{\mathscr{H}}^{k}\mathbf{I}^{\nu}
is a semi-simple perverse sheaf.

(ii)

If
\varepsilon=\eta/|\eta|
, then the complex
\mathbf{I}^{\nu}
is supported on the nilpotent cone, i.e.
\mathbf{I}^{\nu}\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}^{\mathrm{nil}}\right)
.

Démonstration.

The statement
(i)
follows from the Beĭlinson–Bernstein–Deligne–Gabber decomposition theorem and the purity of
\mathscr{C}

[
16
, 1.4]
. The statement
(ii)
follows from to the assumption that
\varepsilon=\eta/|\eta|
and (
).
∎

Define the following space

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{H}}=\prod_{\nu^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\bigoplus_{\nu\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\Ext_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}^{*}\left(\mathbf{I}^{\nu^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{\nu}\right)_{0}.

Recall that the index
0
means the completion at the augmentation ideal
\mathrm{H}^{>0}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}

cf.

subsection 1.16
. The Yoneda product gives a ring structure on the space
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
. We will introduce a topology on it in
section 8
.

Remark 5.5
.

Although the set
\Xi
(and hence
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}
) is infinite, there is only a finite number of possibilities for the subspace
\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{\eta}
for
\nu\in\Xi
, on which depends the functor
\Ind_{\nu}
; therefore, there is only a finite number of isomorphism classes of the complexes
\mathbf{I}^{\nu}
. However, we needed to take the sum over the infinite set
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}
in order to construct an action of the dDAHA
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
on it. This phenomenon was first observed in
[
24
, 3.4]
and was used to study to the dimension of simple
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
-modules.

5.6
Graded affine Hecke algebras and
\Phi_{J}

Let
J\subsetneq\Delta_{\xi}
. We have a parabolic subgroup
\left(\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J},J\right)
of the canonical relative Weyl group
\left(\mathcal{W}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi}\right)
. We define
\mathbf{H}_{\xi,J}
to the subalgebra of
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
generated by
\mathbf{S}_{\xi}
and
\{s_{a}\}_{a\in J}
. As vector space, it admits a decomposition
\mathbf{H}_{\xi,J}\cong\mathbf{k}\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J}\otimes\mathbf{S}_{\xi}
. In the case where
J=\emptyset
, it recovers the polynomial algebra
\mathbf{H}_{\xi,\emptyset}=\mathbf{S}_{\xi}
.

Recall the set
\Xi_{J}=\partial_{J}\Xi\subset\mathfrak{F}(\mathbb{E}^{M})
of
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-facets of (relative) type
J
introduced in
subsection 4.17
. For each
\sigma\in\Xi_{J}
, we define
\Xi^{\sigma}=\left\{\nu\in\Xi\;;\;\partial_{J}\nu=\sigma\right\}
, so that there is a partition
\Xi=\bigsqcup_{\sigma\in\Xi_{J}}\Xi^{\sigma}
. In other words,
\Xi^{\sigma}
is the subset of
\Xi
consisting of those
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-alcoves whose closure contains
\sigma
. The stabiliser
W_{\xi,\sigma}=\Stab_{W_{\xi}}(\sigma)
acts simply transitively on
\Xi^{\sigma}
. Let
\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\sigma}=W_{\xi,\sigma,\mathbf{x}}\backslash\Xi^{\sigma}
, where
W_{\xi,\sigma,\mathbf{x}}=W_{\xi,\sigma}\cap W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}
.

With the datum
(L^{\sigma}_{\diamond},\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{\diamond,*},\xi)
, we are in the situation of
subsection 1.8
. The group
\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J}
can be identified with the canonical relative Weyl group of
L^{\sigma}_{\diamond}
with respect to
\xi
. Moreover, by
subsection 4.9

(ii)
, the vector space
\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond}
coincides with
\mathbf{X}_{*}(Z_{M_{\diamond}})_{\mathbf{Q}}
and
\Xi^{\sigma}
can be identified with the set denoted by
\Xi
in
subsection 1.9
. Choosing any
\nu_{0}\in\Xi^{\sigma}
, we have an isomorphism
(\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond},\Delta_{\xi})\cong\left(\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond},\Delta_{\xi}^{\nu_{0}}\right)
. Under this isomorphism, the based root subsystem
(\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond},J)
is sent to a based root system
\left(\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond},\Delta_{\xi}^{\sigma}\right)
with
\Delta^{\sigma}_{\xi}\subset\Delta^{\nu_{0}}_{\xi}
. Thus we can identify the subalgebra
\mathbf{H}_{\xi,J}\subset\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
with the graded affine Hecke algebra (which was denoted by
\underline{\mathbf{H}}_{\xi}
in
subsection 1.1
) attached to
(\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond},\Delta^{\sigma}_{\xi})
via the isomorphism
(\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\diamond},\Delta_{\xi})\cong(\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond},\Delta_{\xi}^{\nu_{0}})
.

For
\sigma\in\Xi_{J}
, define the parabolic version of
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
attached to
\sigma
:

\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}=\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}},\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\sigma}}\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{\diamond,0,q}}\left(\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C}_{\eta},\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}\right)_{0}.

Recall the element
\mathbf{x}=(\lambda_{0}/m,1)\in{\mathbb{A}}
. We consider the equivariant localisation by action of the fractional cocharacter
(\mathbf{x},\eta/2m)=(2\lambda_{0},2m,\eta)/2m\in\mathbf{X}_{*}(T_{\diamond}\times{\mathbf{C}^{\times}_{q}})
. Notice that
(\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma})^{(\mathbf{x},\eta/2m)}=(\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma})^{(2\lambda_{0},2m,\eta)}=\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{\eta}
. The constructions of
subsection 1.1
can thus be applied
**
**

**

Our group is now
L^{\sigma}_{\diamond}
with maximal torus
{T_{\diamond}}
. The
\mathbf{Z}
-grading on
\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}
is given by
\lambda_{\diamond}=(\lambda_{0},m)
with
\delta({\lambda_{\diamond}})=m
. Notice that the completion is taken at
(\mathbf{x},\eta/2m)
instead of
(\lambda_{\diamond},\eta/2)
. It is fine because they are proportional.
to the datum
(L^{\sigma}_{\diamond},\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{\diamond,*},\xi)
. In particular, there is a homomorphism
\Phi_{\sigma}
defined by means of equivariant localisation

(5.7)

which is independent of the choice of
\nu_{0}
, see the proof of
subsection 1.17
.

We set

\displaystyle\Phi_{J}=\left(\Phi_{\sigma}\right)_{\sigma\in\Xi_{J}}:\mathbf{H}_{\xi,J}\to\prod_{\underline{\smash{\sigma}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}_{J}}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma},\quad\text{where $\underline{\smash{\Xi}}_{J}=W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}\backslash\Xi_{J}$}.

5.8
Construction of
\Phi

Let
K\subseteq J\subsetneq\Delta_{\xi}
. Given
\sigma\in\Xi_{J}
, we let
\Xi_{K}^{\sigma}=\left\{\tau\in\Xi_{K}\;;\;\partial_{J}\tau=\sigma\right\}
. For each
\sigma\in\Xi_{J}
,
\tau\in\Xi^{\sigma}_{K}
and
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi^{\tau}
, there is a homomorphism given by the functoriality of
\Ind^{\sigma}_{\tau}
:

\displaystyle\Ext^{*}_{L^{\tau}_{0,\diamond,q}}\left(\Ind^{\tau}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\tau}_{\nu}\mathscr{C}\right)_{0}\to\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{0,\diamond,q}}\left(\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu}\mathscr{C}\right)_{0}.

Summing over
\underline{\smash{\nu}},\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\tau}
, we get

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}=\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}},\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\tau}}\Ext^{*}_{L^{\tau}_{0,\diamond,q}}\left(\Ind^{\tau}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\tau}_{\nu}\mathscr{C}\right)_{0}\to\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}},\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\tau}}\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{0,\diamond,q}}\left(\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu}\mathscr{C}\right)_{0}.

On the other hand, the partition
\Xi^{\sigma}=\bigsqcup_{\tau\in\Xi^{\sigma}_{K}}\Xi^{\tau}
yields an inclusion of subring

\displaystyle\prod_{\underline{\smash{\tau}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\sigma}_{K}}\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}},\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\tau}}\Ext^{*}_{L^{\sigma}_{0,\diamond,q}}\left(\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu}\mathscr{C}\right)_{0}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}.

Combining the above two maps, we obtain an ring homomorphism

\displaystyle\psi^{\sigma}_{K}:\prod_{\tau\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\sigma}_{K}}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}.

subsection 1.17
can be reformulated as follows :

Similarly, using the functoriality of the spiral induction
\Ind_{\sigma}
for
\underline{\smash{\sigma}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}_{J}
, we have a ring homomorphism

\displaystyle\psi_{J}:\prod_{\underline{\smash{\sigma}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}_{J}}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}.

The transitivity of spiral induction with parabolic induction (
) implies that
\psi_{K}=\psi_{J}\circ\left(\prod_{\underline{\smash{\sigma}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}_{J}}\psi^{\sigma}_{K}\right)
for
K\subset J
.

Theorem 5.10
.

There is a unique ring homomorphism

\displaystyle\Phi:\mathbf{H}_{\xi}\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}

such that for each
J\subsetneq\Delta_{\xi}
, the following diagram commutes
