\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Démonstration.

Taking inductive limit over the partially ordered set
\left(\left\{J\subsetneq\Delta_{\xi}\right\},\subseteq\right)
, we obtain by
subsection 5.8
a ring homomorphism

\varinjlim_{J}\mathbf{H}_{\xi,J}\xrightarrow{(\psi_{J}\circ\Phi_{J})_{J}}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}.

It suffices to show that the natural ring homomorphism induced by inclusion of parabolic subalgebras

\iota:\varinjlim_{J\subsetneq\Delta_{\xi}}\mathbf{H}_{\xi,J}\to\mathbf{H}_{\xi}

is a ring isomorphism. The map
\iota
is surjective because each generator of
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
lies in a parabolic subalgebra. Observe the following :

(i)

Each of the defining relations of
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
involves at most two elements from the set of simple reflections
\left\{s_{a}\right\}_{a\in\Delta_{\xi}}
.

(ii)

In the case where
\#\Delta_{\xi}=2
, there is no braid relation between the two simple reflections.

Each defining relation of
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
must therefore lie in the parabolic subalgebra
\mathbf{H}_{\xi,J}
for some
J\subsetneq\Delta_{\xi}
. It follows that
\iota
is also injective.
∎

So far, we have very little information about the maps
\psi_{J}
and
\Phi
. We will show in
subsection 7.17
that
\psi_{J}
is injective and we will determine its image.

6
Geometry of the Steinberg varieties

We have explained in
subsection 2.7
that
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}_{\aff}\cong\bigsqcup_{\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\mathcal{T}^{\nu}
is an infinite disjoint union of smooth algebraic varieties. In this section, we study the fixed points
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}_{\aff}
of the Steinberg variety
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}=\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}\times_{\mathfrak{g}_{\aff}}\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}
. We describe the stratification of
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}^{\lambda_{\diamond,q}}
by the relative position of pairs of spirals. The exposition below will be given purely in terms of finite dimensional varieties and the ind-schemes such as
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}
and
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{\aff}
will only serve heuristically.

The main result
subsection 6.17
states that the filtration by Bruhat order on the Steinberg varieties is a filtration by closed subvarieties and that the convolution product on the Steinberg variety respects the filtration by Bruhat order. This will allow us to define a filtration on the convolution algebra in the next section
section 7
.

We keep the assumptions of the previous sections.

6.1
Double quotient

Recall that
I=I_{\xi}
is the type of
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-chambers. We recollect some basic combinatorial properties about the double quotient
\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Recall that
W_{\xi}
acts on
\Xi
and
W
acts on
\mathfrak{F}_{I}
.

(i)

There is a canonical inclusion
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
.

(ii)

There is a canonical bijection

\displaystyle W\backslash\left(\mathfrak{F}_{I}\times\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}

which induces a bijection

\displaystyle W_{\xi}\backslash\left(\Xi\times\Xi\right)

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{W}_{\xi}.

Démonstration.

The inclusion in assertion
(i)
is from
subsection 4.17
, given by the shortest representatives. Choosing any
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
, we define

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}

\displaystyle\to W\backslash\left(\mathfrak{F}_{I}\times\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{I}w\mathcal{W}_{I}

\displaystyle\mapsto W\left(\partial_{I}\kappa,w^{\kappa}\partial_{I}\kappa\right).

Since
\mathfrak{F}_{I}\cong\mathfrak{A}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
as
W
-set, the above map is a bijection and does not depend on the choice of
\kappa
. The second statement follows, since both
W_{\xi}
and
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
act simply transitively on
\Xi
.
∎

Definition 6.4
.

The image of any pair
(\nu,\nu^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{F}_{I}\times\mathfrak{F}_{I}
under the map

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{I}\times\mathfrak{F}_{I}\to W\backslash\left(\mathfrak{F}_{I}\times\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)\cong\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}

is called the
relative position
of
\nu
and
\nu^{\prime}
. The relative position is called
good
if it is in the image of the inclusion
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
and
bad
otherwise.

We introduce a partial order on the double quotient
\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
. For
w\in\mathcal{W}
we denote by
[w]=\mathcal{W}_{I}w\mathcal{W}_{I}\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
the double coset containing
w
.

Proposition 6.5
.

The following statements hold :

(i)

Each double coset
[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
has a maximal (resp. minimal) representative in
\mathcal{W}
with respect to the Bruhat order, denoted by
\max([w])\in\mathcal{W}
(resp.
\min([w])
). The map
w\mapsto\max([w])
(resp.
w\mapsto\min([w])
is order-preserving.

(ii)

Define partial orders
\leq_{\max}
and
\leq_{\min}
on
\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
by

\displaystyle\leq_{\max}[w^{\prime}]\Leftrightarrow\max([w])\leq\max([w^{\prime}])

\displaystyle[w]\leq_{\min}[w^{\prime}]\Leftrightarrow\min([w])\leq\min([w^{\prime}]).

Then
\leq_{\max}
and
\leq_{\min}
coincide.

(iii)

The restriction of
\leq_{\max}
(resp.
\leq_{\min}
) on
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}\subset\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
refines the Bruhat order on
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
.

Démonstration.

For
w\in\mathcal{W}
, let
p(w)\in\mathcal{W}
be the maximal element in
\mathcal{W}_{I}w
and let
q(w)\in\mathcal{W}
be the maximal element in
w\mathcal{W}_{I}
. It is clear that
p:\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{W}
and
q:\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{W}
preserve the order.

Fix
[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
. Replacing
w
with
q(p(w))
, we may suppose that
p(w)=w=q(w)
. Given any
w^{\prime}\in[w]
, pick
v\in\mathcal{W}_{I}w^{\prime}\cap w\mathcal{W}_{I}\neq\emptyset
. It follows that
v\leq w
so
w^{\prime}\leq p(w^{\prime})=p(v)\leq p(w)=w
. Hence
w
is maximal in
[w]
. Moreover, we see that
\max([w])=q(p(w))
. Similarly, the minimal element exists in
[w]
.

Since
p
and
q
preserve the Bruhat order, so does the map
w\mapsto\max([w])
. Similarly, the map
w\mapsto\min([w])
also preserves the Bruhat order. Hence
\leq_{\min}
and
\leq_{\max}
coincides.

The last statement follows from
subsection 4.17
.
∎

We say that a subset
C
of a partially ordered set
(S,\leq)
is an
ideal
if
x\in C
and
y\leq x
implies
y\in C
. The combinatorial results below follow from
subsection 6.1
and are easy to prove.

Corollary 6.6
.

The following statements hold

(i)

If
\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{W}
satisfies
\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{W}_{I}\mathcal{I}\mathcal{W}_{I}
, then
\mathcal{I}
is an ideal of
(\mathcal{W},\leq)
if and only if
\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{I}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
is an ideal of
\left(\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I},\leq_{\max}\right)
.

(ii)

If
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{J}
are ideals of
(\mathcal{W},\leq)
, then so is
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}
.

(iii)

If
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{J}
are ideals of
\left(\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I},\leq_{\max}\right)
, then so is the product

\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}=\left\{[ww^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}\;;\;[w]\in\mathcal{I}\,,\;[w^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{J}\right\}.

∎

6.7
The fixed-point components
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}

Consider the space
\mathcal{P}
of spirals which are
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}
-conjugate to
\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{*}
for some
\nu\in\Xi
. This space is isomorphic to an infinite disjoint union of partial flag varieties of
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\cong\bigsqcup_{\underline{\smash{\nu}}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}}\mathcal{P}^{\nu},\quad\mathcal{P}^{\nu}=G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}/P^{\nu}_{0}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}

given by
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}/P^{\nu}_{0}\ni gP^{\nu}_{0}\mapsto\Ad_{g}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{*}\in\mathcal{P}
.

For
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
, we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=\mathcal{P}^{\nu}\times\mathcal{P}^{\nu^{\prime}}.

Recall that by
subsection 4.9
, the Weyl group
W({G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}},T)
is isomorphic to the stabiliser
W_{\mathbf{x}}=\Stab_{W}(\mathbf{x})
. Let
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
. By the Bruhat decomposition, the orbits of the
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
-action on
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
are in canonical bijection with the orbits of diagonal left translation of
W_{\mathbf{x}}
on
\left(W_{\mathbf{x}}/W_{\mathbf{x},\nu}\right)\times\left(W_{\mathbf{x}}/W_{\mathbf{x},\nu^{\prime}}\right)
, where
W_{\mathbf{x},\nu}=W_{\mathbf{x}}\cap W_{\nu}
.

Define the map of
relative position

\Pi_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}:\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
by

\displaystyle G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\backslash\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\cong W_{\mathbf{x}}\backslash\left(\left(W_{\mathbf{x}}/W_{\mathbf{x},\nu}\right)\times\left(W_{\mathbf{x}}/W_{\mathbf{x},\nu^{\prime}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\rightarrow W\backslash\left(\mathfrak{F}_{I}\times\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)\cong\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}

\displaystyle W_{\mathbf{x}}\cdot(uW_{\mathbf{x},\nu},vW_{\mathbf{x},\nu^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\mapsto W\cdot(u\nu,v\nu^{\prime}),

the last isomorphism given by
subsection 6.1
(ii)
. We define for each
w\in\mathcal{W}
and each
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
the locally closed subvariety

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}=\Pi^{-1}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}([w])\subset\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},

equipped with the reduced subscheme structure. Obviously,
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}=\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w^{\prime}}
if
[w]=[w^{\prime}]
. Similarly, for each ideal
\mathcal{I}
of
(\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I},\leq_{\max})
, we put

\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}=\Pi_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{I})=\bigcup_{[y]\in\mathcal{I}}\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{y}.

The following statements are analogues of standard results about
(B,N)
-pairs and convolution on flag manifolds. The arguments are similar to the classical ones using the Demazure desingularisation.

Lemma 6.8
.

The following statements hold :

(i)

If
\mathcal{I}
is an ideal of
(\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I},\leq_{\max})
, then
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\leq\mathcal{I}}
is closed in
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
.

(ii)

If
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{J}
are ideals of
\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
, then the image of the projection

\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}\times_{\mathcal{P}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{X}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{J}}\to\mathcal{X}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}},\quad(gP^{\nu}_{0},g^{\prime}P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0},g^{\prime\prime}P^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{0})\mapsto(gP^{\nu}_{0},g^{\prime\prime}P^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{0}).

is contained in
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}}
, where
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
is the product of
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{J}
.

Démonstration.

Consider first the scheme
\mathcal{B}
which parametrises minimal spirals. For each minimal spiral
\mathfrak{p}_{*}
, we can find
g\in G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
such that
T\subset gP_{0}g^{-1}
. Since by
subsection 4.5
, the minimal
T
-stable spirals are parametrised by
\mathfrak{A}
, it follows that
\mathcal{B}
is isomorphic to an infinite disjoint union of flag varieties of
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}\cong\bigsqcup_{\underline{\smash{\kappa}}\in\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{A}}}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa},\quad\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}=G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}/P^{\kappa}_{0},\quad\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{A}}}=W_{\mathbf{x}}\backslash\mathfrak{A},

given by
\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}\ni gP^{\kappa}_{0}\mapsto\Ad_{g}\mathfrak{p}^{\kappa}_{*}\in\mathcal{B}
. Define
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}=\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}\times\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}
. We can define as above a map of relative position
\Pi_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}:\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{W}
.

Step 1
.

Let
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
be an alcove and
w,s\in\mathcal{W}
with
\ell(s)=1
. Denote
\kappa^{\prime}=w^{-1}\kappa
and
\kappa^{\prime\prime}=s^{-1}\kappa^{\prime}
. We consider the image of the map

(6.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}_{w}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{s}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime\prime}}.

Case 1.

Suppose that
\ell(ws)=\ell(w)+1
. Then we have
P^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{0}=\left(P^{\kappa}_{0}\cap P^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{0}\right)\left(P^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{0}\cap P^{\kappa}_{0}\right)
and hence

\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}_{w}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{s}\cong\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{ws}.

Case 2.

Suppose that
\ell(ws)=\ell(w)-1
. By the previous case, we have
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{ws}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}}\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime},\kappa^{\prime}}_{s}\cong\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}_{w}
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}_{w}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{s}\cong\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{ws}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}}\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime},\kappa^{\prime}}_{s}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{s}

Using the fact that image of
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime},\kappa^{\prime}}_{s}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{s}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}}
lies in
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{e}\cup\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{s}
, we deduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{ws}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}}\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime},\kappa^{\prime}}_{s}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{s}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{ws}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}}\left(\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{e}\cup\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{s}\right)\cong\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{ws}\cup\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{w}.

Thus, the image of (
) lies in
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{ws}\cup\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{w}
in both cases.

Now we show that if
\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{W}
is an ideal and
\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{A}
are alcoves, then
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}=\Pi_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{I})
is a proper algebraic variety. Let
y\in\mathcal{I}
be such that
\underline{\smash{\kappa}}^{\prime}=y^{-1}\underline{\smash{\kappa}}
and pick a reduced decomposition
y=s_{1}\cdots s_{r}
. For each
i
, put
\kappa_{i}=s_{i}\cdots s_{1}\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
and consider
D_{i}=\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{i-1},\kappa_{i}}_{e}\cup\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{i-1},\kappa_{i}}_{s_{i}}
.

Step 2
.

We show that
D_{i}
is a proper variety for each
i
.

Case 1.

If
\underline{\smash{\kappa}}_{i-1}\neq\underline{\smash{\kappa}}_{i}
, then
\mathcal{Y}_{e}^{\kappa_{i-1},\kappa_{i}}=\emptyset
and

\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{i-1},\kappa_{i}}_{s_{i}}={G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}\cdot\left(P^{\kappa_{i-1}}_{0},P^{\kappa_{i}}_{0}\right)\subset\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{i-1},\kappa_{i}}.

However, since
\kappa_{i-1}
and
\kappa_{i}
are adjacent in
{\mathbb{A}}
, the condition
\underline{\smash{\kappa}}_{i-1}\neq\underline{\smash{\kappa}}_{i}
implies that the hyperplane separating
\kappa_{i-1}
and
\kappa_{i}
does not contain
\mathbf{x}\in{\mathbb{A}}
. Since by
subsection 4.9
, the root system
R({G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}},T)
can be identified with the set of affine roots which vanish at
\mathbf{x}
, we see that
P^{\kappa_{i-1}}_{0}=P^{\kappa_{i}}_{0}
as subgroup of
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
. Therefore, we have
\Stab_{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}\left(P^{\kappa_{i-1}}_{0},P^{\kappa_{i}}_{0}\right)=P^{\kappa_{i-1}}_{0}
. It follows that
D_{i}=\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{i-1},\kappa_{i}}_{s_{i}}\cong G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}/P^{\kappa_{i-1}}_{0}
is a proper variety.

Case 2.

If
\underline{\smash{\kappa}}_{i-1}=\underline{\smash{\kappa}}_{i}
, then
s_{i}^{\kappa_{i-1}}
lies in
W_{\mathbf{x}}
, so there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{i-1},\kappa_{i}}\cong\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{i},\kappa_{i}}
. Let
\dot{s}^{\kappa_{i}}_{i}\in N_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}}(T)
be a lifting of
s_{i}^{\kappa_{i-1}}
. Then
D_{i}
can be described by

\displaystyle D_{i}\cong\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{i},\kappa_{i}}_{e}\cup\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{i},\kappa_{i}}_{s_{i}}={G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}\cdot\left(P^{\kappa_{i}}_{0},P^{\kappa_{i}}_{0}\right)\cup{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}\cdot\left(P^{\kappa_{i}}_{0},\dot{s}^{\kappa_{i}}_{i}P^{\kappa_{i}}_{0}\right).

We see that the projection
D_{i}\xrightarrow{\pr_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{i}}
is a
\mathbf{P}^{1}
-bundle. Hence
D_{i}
is proper.

Step 3
.

Now consider the convolution product

\displaystyle D_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{1}}}\cdots\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{r-1}}}D_{r}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{r}}

\displaystyle\left(g_{1}P^{\kappa_{0}}_{0},g_{2}P^{\kappa_{1}}_{0},\ldots,g_{r+1}P^{\kappa_{r}}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\mapsto\left(g_{1}P^{\kappa_{0}}_{0},g_{r+1}P^{\kappa_{r}}_{0}\right)

We show by induction on
i
that the image of
D_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{1}}}\cdots\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{i-1}}}D_{i}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{i}}
contains
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{i}}_{s_{1}\cdots s_{i}}
and lies in
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{i}}_{\leq s_{1}\cdots s_{i}}
. It is trivial for
i=0
. Suppose
i>0
and that the statement is proven for
i-1
, so that we have
D_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{1}}}\cdots\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{i-2}}}D_{i-1}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{i-1}}_{\leq s_{1}\cdots s_{i-1}}
. Applying Step 1, we have

\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{i-1}}_{\leq s_{1}\cdots s_{i-1}}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{i-1}}}D_{i}=\bigcup_{w^{\prime}\leq s_{1}\cdots s_{i-1}}\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{i-1}}_{w^{\prime}}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{i-1}}}D_{i}\to\bigcup_{w^{\prime}\leq s_{1}\cdots s_{i-1}}(\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{i}}_{w^{\prime}}\cup\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{i}}_{w^{\prime}s_{i}}).

Since
w^{\prime}\leq s_{1}\cdots s_{i-1}
implies
w^{\prime}\leq s_{1}\cdots s_{i}
and
w^{\prime}s_{i}\leq s_{1}\cdots s_{i}
, the image of the composition

D_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{1}}}\cdots\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{i-1}}}D_{i}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{i-1}}_{\leq s_{1}\cdots s_{i-1}}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{i-1}}}D_{i}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{i}}

lies in
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{i}}_{\leq s_{1}\cdots s_{i}}
. The case 1 of Step 1 also implies that
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{i}}_{s_{1}\cdots s_{i}}
lies in the image.

Step 4
.

Since
D_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{1}}}\cdots\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa_{r-1}}}D_{r}
is a proper variety, its image in
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{r}}_{\leq s_{1}\cdots s_{r}}
is also proper. We see that,
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{r}}_{y}
is contained in a
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
-invariant proper subvariety of
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa_{0},\kappa_{r}}_{\leq y}
. Letting
y
run over all elements of
\mathcal{I}
with
y\underline{\smash{\kappa}}^{\prime}=\underline{\smash{\kappa}}
, we see that
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}
is covered by a finite number of proper subvarieties of
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}
. Hence
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}
is proper.

Step 5
.

Applying Step 1, standard arguments of
(B,N)
-pairs show that the image of

\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}}\times\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{J}}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime\prime}}

is contained in
\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}}
.

Step 6
.

We prove the statement
(i)
. For
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
, pick alcoves
\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{A}
such that
\partial_{I}\kappa=\nu
and
\partial_{I}\kappa^{\prime}=\nu^{\prime}
. The projection
f:\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
satisfies the property that
f^{-1}(\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w})=\bigcup_{y\in[w]}\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}_{y}
for each
[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
. Since
\mathcal{I}
is an ideal of
(\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I},\leq_{\max})
, by
subsection 6.1
, the set
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}=\left\{w\in\mathcal{W}\;;\;[w]\in\mathcal{I}\right\}
is an ideal of
\mathcal{W}
. Thus
f^{-1}(\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}})=\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}}
is proper by Step 4 and so is the image
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}=f(\mathcal{Y}^{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}})
proper. Hence
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}
is closed in
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
.

Similarly, one can prove
(ii)
by descending the corresponding statement about
\mathcal{Y}
in Step 5.

∎

6.10
Good strata of
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}

For general
[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
, the stratum
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}=\Pi^{-1}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}[w]
may not be a single
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
-orbit. However, the following lemma shows that it is the case for those strata
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}
with
w\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
and
w\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}=\underline{\smash{\nu}}
. As we will see in
section 7
, only these strata will have contribution to the extension algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
and
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
.

(i)

The stratum
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}
contains a finite number of
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
-orbits.

(ii)

If
w\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
, then we have
\#\left(G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\backslash\Pi_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}^{-1}\left[w\right]\right)\leq 1
. Moreover, the equality holds if and only if
\underline{\smash{\nu}}=w\underline{\smash{\nu^{\prime}}}
.

Démonstration.

The assertion
(i)
follows from the fact that
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\backslash\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
is finite.

We prove
(ii)
. Suppose that
w\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
and
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
such that
\underline{\smash{\nu}}=w\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}
. We may assume that
\nu=w\nu^{\prime}
since
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
and
\Pi_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
depends only on the
W_{\xi,\mathbf{x}}
-orbits
\underline{\smash{\nu}}
and
\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}
. Let
w^{\nu}\in W_{\xi}
be the image of
w
under the isomorphism
\left(\mathcal{W}_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi}\right)\cong\left(W_{\xi},\Delta_{\xi}^{\nu}\right)
so that
\nu^{\prime}=w^{\nu}\nu

cf.

subsection 6.1
. It suffices to show that the map

\displaystyle\varphi:W_{\mathbf{x}}\backslash\left(\left(W_{\mathbf{x}}/W_{\mathbf{x},\nu}\right)\times\left(W_{\mathbf{x}}/W_{\mathbf{x},\nu^{\prime}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\to W\backslash\left(\mathfrak{F}_{I}\times\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)

\displaystyle W_{\mathbf{x}}\cdot(u,u^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mapsto W\cdot(u\nu,u^{\prime}w^{\nu}\nu)

is injective over
W\cdot\left(\nu,w^{\nu}\nu\right)\in W\backslash\left(\mathfrak{F}_{I}\times\mathfrak{F}_{I}\right)
.
Assume we have
u,u^{\prime}\in W_{\mathbf{x}}
such that
\left(uW_{\mathbf{x},\nu},u^{\prime}W_{\mathbf{x},\nu^{\prime}}\right)
is sent to
W\cdot\left(\nu,w^{\nu}\nu\right)
, in other words

\displaystyle W\cdot\left(u\nu,u^{\prime}w^{\nu}\nu\right)=W\cdot\left(\nu,w^{\nu}\nu\right).

We shall prove that
W_{\mathbf{x}}\cdot\left(u\nu,u^{\prime}w^{\nu}\nu\right)=W_{\mathbf{x}}\cdot\left(\nu,w^{\nu}\nu\right)
.

Indeed, as

\displaystyle W\cdot\left(\nu,w^{\nu}\nu\right)=W\cdot\left(u\nu,u^{\prime}w^{\nu}\nu\right)=W\cdot\left(\nu,u^{-1}u^{\prime}w^{\nu}\nu\right)

there exists
q\in W_{\nu}=W_{M}
such that
qw^{\nu}\nu=u^{-1}u^{\prime}w^{\nu}\nu
, or equivalently

\displaystyle\left(w^{\nu}\right)^{-1}q^{-1}u^{-1}u^{\prime}w^{\nu}\in W_{\nu}.

Since
w^{\nu}
normalises
W_{\nu}
, we obtain
(w^{\nu})^{-1}u^{-1}u^{\prime}w^{\nu}\in W_{\nu}
and hence

\displaystyle W_{\mathbf{x}}\cdot\left(u\nu,u^{\prime}w^{\nu}\nu\right)

\displaystyle=W_{\mathbf{x}}\cdot\left(\nu,u^{-1}u^{\prime}w^{\nu}\nu\right)=W_{\mathbf{x}}\cdot\left(\nu,w^{\nu}((w^{\nu})^{-1}u^{-1}u^{\prime}w^{\nu})\nu\right)=W_{\mathbf{x}}\cdot(\nu,w^{\nu}\nu),

which concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.12
.

For each
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
and each
w\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
such that
w\underline{\smash{\nu^{\prime}}}=\underline{\smash{\nu}}
, there is an isomorphism of
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}
-schemes

Démonstration.

By
subsection 6.10
,
\mathcal{X}_{w}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
is a
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
-orbit. The proposition results from the fact that the
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}
-action on
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,w^{-1}\nu}_{w}
satisfies

\Stab_{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}\left(P_{0}^{\nu},P_{0}^{w^{-1}\nu}\right)=P_{0}^{\nu}\cap P_{0}^{w^{-1}\nu}.\qed

There is a canonical bijection

P^{\nu}_{0}\backslash G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}/P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}\cong G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\backslash\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\quad P^{\nu}_{0}gP^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}\mapsto{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}\cdot(P^{\nu}_{0},gP^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}).

The following technical lemma will be used in the proof of
subsection 7.4
to show that bad orbits do not contribute to the extension algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
.

Proposition 6.13
.

Let
\Omega\in P^{\nu}_{0}\backslash G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}/P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}
be a double coset and let
O_{\Omega}\in G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\backslash\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
be the corresponding orbit. Then
\Pi_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\left(O_{\Omega}\right)\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
if and only if for any
g\in\Omega
, the following natural inclusion

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{N}\cap\Ad_{g}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{N}\right)/\left(\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{N}\cap\Ad_{g}\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{N}\right)\to\Ad_{g}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{N}/\Ad_{g}\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{N}

is an isomorphism for each
N\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Démonstration.

Let
g\in\Omega
. According to
[
18
, 5.1]
, there is a splitting
\mathfrak{l}_{*}
of
\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{*}
and a splitting
\mathfrak{l}^{\prime}_{*}
of
\Ad_{g}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{*}
such that
L^{\nu}_{0}=\exp\left(\mathfrak{l}_{0}\right)
and
L^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}=\exp\left(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime}_{0}\right)
contain a common maximal torus
T^{\prime}
of
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
.

Let
g^{\prime}\in G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
be such that
g^{\prime}T^{\prime}g^{\prime-1}=T
. Choose any alcove
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
such that
\partial_{I}\kappa=\nu
. Then
\Ad_{g^{\prime}}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{*}
and
\Ad_{g^{\prime}g}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{*}
are in
\mathfrak{P}_{T}
and there exist
y,w\in\mathcal{W}
such that
\Ad_{g^{\prime}}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{*}=\mathfrak{p}^{y^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}
and
\Ad_{g^{\prime}g}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{*}=\mathfrak{p}^{y^{\kappa}w^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}
. From the definition of
\Pi_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
, we see that
\Pi_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\left(O_{\Omega}\right)=[w]
. Moreover,
[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
if and only if
y^{\kappa}\nu
and
y^{\kappa}w^{\kappa}\nu
span the same affine subspace, which is equivalent to that
\mathfrak{l}^{y^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}=\mathfrak{l}^{y^{\kappa}w^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}
. Using the Levi decomposition
\mathfrak{p}^{y^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}=\mathfrak{l}^{y^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{y^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{y^{\kappa}w^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}=\mathfrak{l}^{y^{\kappa}w^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{y^{\kappa}w^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}
, we see that
\mathfrak{l}^{y^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}=\mathfrak{l}^{y^{\kappa}w^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}
if and only if the obvious map

(6.14)

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{p}^{y^{\kappa}\nu}_{N}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{y^{\kappa}w^{\kappa}\nu}_{N}\right)/\left(\mathfrak{u}^{y^{\kappa}\nu}_{N}\cap\mathfrak{u}^{y^{\kappa}w^{\kappa}\nu}_{N}\right)\to\mathfrak{p}^{y^{\kappa}w^{\kappa}\nu}_{N}/\mathfrak{u}^{y^{\kappa}w^{\kappa}\nu}_{N}

is an isomorphism for all
N\in\mathbf{Z}
. Taking into account that
\Ad_{g^{\prime}}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{*}=\mathfrak{p}^{y^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}
and
\Ad_{g^{\prime}g}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{*}=\mathfrak{p}^{y^{\kappa}w^{\kappa}\nu}_{*}
, the condition that the map (
) is an isomorphism is equivalent to that the map

(6.15)

\displaystyle\left(\Ad_{g^{\prime}}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{N}\cap\Ad_{g^{\prime}g}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{N}\right)/\left(\Ad_{g^{\prime}}\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{N}\cap\Ad_{g^{\prime}g}\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{N}\right)\to\Ad_{g^{\prime}g}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{N}/\Ad_{g^{\prime}g}\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{N}

is an isomorphism for all
N\in\mathbf{Z}
. We obtain the desired equivalent condition by applying
\Ad_{g^{\prime-1}}
to the map (
).
∎

Remark 6.16
.

In the terminology of
[
18
, 5.2]
, if a double coset
\Omega\in P_{0}^{\nu}\backslash G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}/P_{0}^{\nu^{\prime}}
satisfies the condition of
subsection 6.10
, it is called
good
. It is called
bad
otherwise.

6.17
Steinberg varieties

For each
\nu\in\Xi
, we introduce a variety

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\nu}=G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\times^{P_{0}^{\nu}}\left(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}\right).

It is a smooth variety equipped with an action of
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}
, where the factor
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}}
acts by multiplication on the left, and is, up to
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}
-equivariant isomorphism, independent of the choice of
\nu
in its
W_{\mathbf{x}}
-conjugacy class. There is a stack morphism

f:[\mathcal{T}^{\nu}/{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}]\cong[\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}/P^{\nu}_{0,\diamond,q}]\to\left[\mathrm{O}_{\eta}/{M_{0,\diamond,q}}\right].

Let
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}=\mathscr{C}\mid_{\mathrm{O}_{\eta}}
. Define
\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}=f^{*}\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
. It is a
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}
-equivariant local system on
\mathcal{T}^{\nu}
. If we put

a^{\nu}:\mathcal{T}^{\nu}=G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\times^{P_{0}^{\nu}}\left(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}\right)\to\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}},\qquad a^{\nu}(g,z)=\Ad_{g}z,

then we recover the induced complex
a^{\nu}_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}=\Ind_{\nu}\mathscr{C}=\mathbf{I}^{\nu}
introduced in
subsection 5.3
.

For
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
, we take the fibred product of
a^{\nu}
and
a^{\nu^{\prime}}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=\mathcal{T}^{\nu}\times_{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}.

There is a canonical
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}
-equivariant map which forgets the Lie algebra component

\displaystyle g:\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}.

For
w\in\mathcal{W}
, we set
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}=g^{-1}(\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w})
. Similarly, for any ideal
\mathcal{I}
of
\left(\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I},\leq_{\max}\right)
, we put
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}=g^{-1}(\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}})
. The following lemma summarises the geometric properties of the Steinberg varieties
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
that are necessary for our analysis of the extension algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
.

Proposition 6.18
.

Let
\nu,\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}\in\Xi
.

(i)

For every ideal
\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
, the subvariety
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}\subset\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
is closed.

(ii)

For every pair of ideals
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
, the image of the projection

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{J}}\to\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}

lies in
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}}
.

(iii)

For every
w\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
such that
w\underline{\smash{\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}}}=\underline{\smash{\underline{\smash{\nu}}}}
, there is an equivariant isomorphism of
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}
-schemes

\displaystyle\hbox to355.6pt{\vbox to57.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 177.79942pt\lower-28.60666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Démonstration.

The first two statements follow from

and the last one follows from

.

7
Convolution algebra

We keep the assumptions of the previous sections.

In this section, we study the extension algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
in terms of convolution algebras. We deduce some properties of convolution algebras from the geometry of Steinberg varieties
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
studied in the previous section.

7.1
Convolution product

Let
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
. We have two projections
q_{1}:\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{T}^{\nu}
and
q_{2}:\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}
. We set

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=\Ext^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathbf{I}^{\nu^{\prime}},\mathbf{I}^{\nu}\right).

We put
\mathscr{K}=\scrHom(q_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}},q_{1}^{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu})
. The Verdier duality yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\cong\Ext^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(q_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}},q_{1}^{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\right)\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\mathscr{K}).

For the reason explained in
subsection 5.3
, the definition of
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
is, up to canonical isomorphism, independent of
\nu
and
\nu^{\prime}
in their respective
W_{\mathbf{x}}
-conjugacy classes.

By the formalism of
[
14
]
and
[
3
]
, for
\nu,\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}\in\Xi
, the Yoneda product

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}

can be described in terms of
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
and
\mathscr{K}
. It is called the
convolution product
. One subtlety of it is that the maps
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
are not proper and
a priori
cohomological convolution product cannot be defined for non-proper maps.

We recall the construction of this convolution product. Since the orbit
\mathrm{O}
is distinguished,
\mathrm{O}_{\eta}
must be open and dense in
\mathfrak{m}_{\eta}

cf.

[
16
, 4.4a]
. We set
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}=G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\times^{P_{0}^{\nu}}\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{\eta}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}\times_{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}
. Let
u^{\nu}:\mathcal{T}^{\nu}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}
and
u^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}:\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
be the open embeddings. We remark that
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}
is smooth and is proper over
\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}
. Let
q_{1}:\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{T}^{\nu}
,
q_{2}:\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}
,
\widehat{q}_{1}:\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}
and
\widehat{q}_{2}:\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu^{\prime}}
be the canonical projections.

By the cleanness of
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
(
[
16
, §4]
), we have
u^{\nu}_{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}=u^{\nu}_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}
. Put
\widehat{\mathscr{K}}=\scrHom\left(\widehat{q}_{2}^{*}u^{\nu^{\prime}}_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}},\widehat{q}_{1}^{!}u^{\nu}_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\right)
, which is a complex on
\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
. We have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{K}}

\displaystyle\cong\scrHom\left(\widehat{q}_{2}^{*}u^{\nu^{\prime}}_{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}},\widehat{q}_{1}^{!}u^{\nu}_{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\right)\cong\scrHom\left(u^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{!}q_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}},u^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{*}q_{1}^{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\cong u^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{*}\scrHom\left(q_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}},q_{1}^{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\right)\cong u^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{*}\mathscr{K}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\mathscr{K}\right)\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\widehat{\mathscr{K}}\right).

It follows that the cleanness of the cuspidal local system
\mathscr{C}
assures that we can safely work on the non-proper version
\mathcal{T}^{\nu}
,
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
and
\mathscr{K}
.

\nu,\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}\in\Xi

There is a sequence of maps

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\mathscr{K}\right)\otimes\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}},\mathscr{K}\right)

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}},t^{!}\left(\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\boxtimes\mathbf{D}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}}\boxtimes\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathbf{D}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{t^{!}\left(\id\to\gamma_{*}\gamma^{*}\right)}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\times\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}},t^{!}\gamma_{*}\left(\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\boxtimes\left(\mathbf{D}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}}\otimes\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}}\right)\boxtimes\mathbf{D}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}},r^{!}\left(\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\boxtimes\left(\mathbf{D}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}}\otimes\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}}\right)\boxtimes\mathbf{D}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbf{D}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\otimes\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\to\mathbf{D}\mathbf{k}}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}},r^{!}\left(\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\boxtimes\mathbf{D}\mathbf{k}\boxtimes\mathbf{D}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}},r^{!}u^{!}\left(\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\boxtimes\mathbf{D}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}},\mu^{!}\mathscr{K}\right)\to\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}},\mathscr{K}\right).

The convolution product
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}
is then defined to be the composite.

Later on, it will be crucial for us to be able to analyse the convolution algebra by the Bruhat filtration
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}
introduced in
subsection 6.17
. Let
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J}
be ideals of
\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
. By
subsection 6.17
(ii)
, we have the map

\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{J}}\to\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}}.

where the vertical arrows are given by adjunction co-units
i_{\mathcal{I}!}i_{\mathcal{I}}^{!}\to\id
.

7.2
Polynomial action

We define the polynomial algebra
\mathbf{S}^{M}=\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{M_{0,\diamond,q}}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta},\mathbf{k})
, graded by the cohomological degree.

Lemma 7.3
.

There is a graded ring isomorphism

\mathbf{S}^{M}\cong\mathbf{k}[\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond}]\otimes\mathbf{k}[u],

where the linear functions
(\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond})^{*}\subset\mathbf{k}[\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond}]
and
u\in\mathbf{k}[u]
are of degree
2
.

Démonstration.

If we choose
e\in\mathrm{O}_{\eta}
and complete it into a
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple
\phi=(e,h,f)
as in
subsection 3.2
, then we have

\mathbf{S}^{M}=\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{M_{0,\diamond,q}}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta},\mathbf{k})\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{Z_{{M_{0,\diamond,q}}}(e)}.

As the orbit
\mathrm{O}
support a cuspidal local system, by
[
16
, 4.2, 4.3]
we have
Z_{{M_{\diamond,q}}}(e)=Z_{{M_{0,\diamond,q}}}(e)
. With the group
Z^{q}_{{M_{\diamond}}}(\phi)
as in (
), we have an isomorphism

\iota:Z_{{M_{\diamond}}}(\phi)\times\mathbf{C}^{\times}\cong Z^{q}_{{M_{\diamond}}}(\phi),\quad\iota(g,q)=(gq^{h},q).

Since
Z_{{M_{\diamond,q}}}(e)\subset Z^{q}_{{M_{\diamond}}}(\phi)
is a maximal reductive subgroup
[
13
, 2.1]
, we deduce that

\mathrm{H}^{*}_{Z_{{M_{\diamond,q}}}(e)}\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{Z^{q}_{{M_{\diamond}}}(\phi)}\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{Z_{{M_{\diamond}}}\times\mathbf{C}^{\times}}\cong\mathbf{k}[\mathbb{E}^{M}_{\diamond}]\otimes\mathbf{k}[u].\qed

Now we define a
(\mathbf{S}^{M},\mathbf{S}^{M})
-bimodule structure on various cohomology groups. For any
\nu\in\Xi
, the stack morphism
[\mathcal{T}^{\nu}/{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}]\to[\mathrm{O}_{\eta}/{M_{0,\diamond,q}}]
induces by pull-back an isomorphism of equivariant cohomology rings :

\mathbf{S}^{M}\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{P_{0,\diamond,q}^{\nu}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta},\mathbf{k})\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{P_{0,\diamond,q}^{\nu}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta},\mathbf{k})\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}(\mathcal{T}^{\nu},\mathbf{k}).

Let
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
and suppose that
i_{V}:V\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
is a
{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}
-stable subvariety. Then the two projections
q_{1}:\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{T}^{\nu}
and
q_{2}:\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}
induce two stack morphisms

[\mathcal{T}^{\nu}/{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}]\xleftarrow{\overline{q}_{1}}[V/{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}]\xrightarrow{\overline{q}_{2}}[\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}/{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}].

The diagonal
(\overline{q}_{1},\overline{q}_{2})
yields a ring homomorphism

\mathbf{S}^{M}\otimes\mathbf{S}^{M}\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}\times{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}(\mathcal{T}^{\nu}\times\mathcal{T}^{\nu},\mathbf{k})\xrightarrow{(\overline{q}_{1},\overline{q}_{2})^{*}}\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}(V,\mathbf{k}),

which defines a graded
(\mathbf{S}^{M},\mathbf{S}^{M})
-bimodule structure on
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}(V,i^{!}_{V}\mathscr{K})
by cup product via
(\overline{q}_{1},\overline{q}_{2})^{*}
.

7.4
Cohomology of strata

We put for
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
and
[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}=\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w},i_{w}^{!}\mathscr{K}\right),

where
i_{w}:\mathcal{Z}_{w}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
is the locally closed immersion. The following lemma determines the “size” of the extension space
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
and is crucial in our calculation of extension algebra.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi
and
[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
.

(i)

If
[w]
is bad, i.e.
[w]\notin\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
, then
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}=0
.

(ii)

If
[w]
is good, i.e.
[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
, and if
w\underline{\smash{\nu^{\prime}}}=\underline{\smash{\nu}}
, then
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}
is a free of rank
1
as left (resp. right) graded
\mathbf{S}^{M}
-module, vanishing in odd degrees.

(iii)

If
[w]
is good but
w\underline{\smash{\nu^{\prime}}}\neq\underline{\smash{\nu}}
, then
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}=0
.

Démonstration.

We prove
(i)
. Suppose that
[w]\notin\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
. Recall that there is a left
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
-action on
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}
with a finite number of orbits. Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}=\bigsqcup_{\Omega}\mathcal{X}_{\Omega}

be the stratification by
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}
-orbits, where
\Omega
is taken over a finite subset of
P^{\nu}_{0}\backslash G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}/P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}
. We define
\mathcal{Z}_{\Omega}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
to be the pre-image of
\mathcal{X}_{\Omega}
under the projection
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
. Notice that all double cosets
\Omega
that appear in this stratification are
bad
in the sense of
[
18
, 5.2]
since
[w]
is not in
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
.

Put

\displaystyle\dot{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}_{\eta}=\left(\mathrm{O}^{\nu}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}\right)\times_{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}

so that
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\cong G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\times^{P_{0}^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}}\dot{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta}
. Moreover, for each
\Omega
we put

\displaystyle\dot{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}_{\eta,\Omega}=\left(\mathrm{O}^{\nu}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}\right)\times_{\mathfrak{g}_{\underline{\smash{\eta}}}}\left(\Omega\times^{P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}}\left(\mathrm{O}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta}\right)\right)\subseteq\dot{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}_{\eta}

then there is a diagram of Cartesian squares

\displaystyle\hbox to218.92pt{\vbox to54.62pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 109.46089pt\lower-27.30946pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Let
i:\mathcal{Z}_{\Omega}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
be the inclusion. Now

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}_{\Omega},i^{!}\mathscr{K}\right)\cong\Ext^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(q_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}},q_{1}^{!}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\right)\cong\Ext^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(q_{1!}q_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}},\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\cong\Ext^{*}_{P_{0,\diamond,q}^{\nu}}\left(r^{*}q_{1!}q_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}},r^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\right)\cong\Ext^{*}_{P_{0,\diamond,q}^{\nu}}\left(q^{\prime}_{1!}r^{\prime*}q_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}},r^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\right).

Taking into account the cuspidality of
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
and the fact that
\Omega
is bad,
cf.

subsection 6.10
and
subsection 6.10
, it is shown in
[
18
, 5.3]
that
q^{\prime}_{1!}r^{\prime*}q_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}}=0
. Thus
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}_{\Omega},i^{!}\mathscr{K}\right)=0
for each
\Omega
. By an argument of long exact sequence of cohomology, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}=\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w},i_{w}^{!}\mathscr{K}\right)=0.

This proves
(i)
.

We turn to
(ii)
. The arguments follow
[
13
, 4.2]
. Suppose that
w\nu^{\prime}=\nu
with
w\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
.
subsection 6.17
gives an isomorphism

G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\times^{P^{\nu}_{0}\cap P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta})\cong\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}.

Let

p_{1}:G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\times^{P^{\nu}_{0}\cap P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta})\to\mathcal{T}^{\nu},\quad p_{2}:G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\times^{P^{\nu}_{0}\cap P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta})\to\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}

be the two projections. Since
\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}
is a local system and since
\mathcal{T}^{\nu}
and
G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\times^{P^{\nu}_{0}\cap P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta})
are smooth varieties, we have
p^{!}_{1}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}\cong p_{1}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu}[2d_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}]
by the Verdier duality, where

d_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=\dim P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}/P^{\nu}_{0}\cap P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}-\dim\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta}/\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta}\in\mathbf{Z}.

Denote by
f:\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta}\to\mathrm{O}_{\eta}
the projection. Then

\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}\cong\Ext^{*+2d_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}(p_{2}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu^{\prime}},p_{1}^{*}\dot{\mathscr{C}}_{\nu})\cong\Ext^{*+2d_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}}_{(P^{\nu}_{0}\cap P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0})_{\diamond,q}}(f^{*}\mathscr{C}_{\eta},f^{*}\mathscr{C}_{\eta})\cong\Ext^{*+2d_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}}_{{M_{0,\diamond,q}}}(\mathscr{C}_{\eta},\mathscr{C}_{\eta}).

Since
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
is irreducible, the last term is isomorphic to
\mathrm{H}^{*+2d_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}}_{{M_{0,\diamond,q}}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta},\mathbf{k})
, which is a graded-free left (resp. right)
\mathbf{S}^{M}
-module of rank
1
shifted by an even degree. By
subsection 7.2
, it vanishes in odd degrees, whence
(ii)
.

Finally, if
w\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
but
w\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}\neq\underline{\smash{\nu}}
, then
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}=\emptyset
by
subsection 6.10
(ii)
, so
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}=0
, whence
(iii)
.
∎

Remark 7.6
.

The special feature of convolution algebra with a cuspidal local system as coefficient is that not every Bruhat cell on the partial flag variety contributes to the cohomology, but only those corresponding to the subset
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}\subset\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
do.
subsection 7.4
roughly says that the convolution algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
and the dDAHA
\mathbf{H}_{\xi}
are of the same size. The proof is adapted from
[
13
, 4.7]
.

7.7
Filtration by Bruhat order

Recall that by
subsection 6.17
,
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}
is a closed subvariety of
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
. Denote by
i_{\mathcal{I}}:\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
the inclusion.

Proposition 7.8
.

For each ideal
\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
, the space

\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}=\mathrm{H}^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}},i_{\mathcal{I}}^{!}\mathscr{K}\right)

is a graded
(\mathbf{S}^{M},\mathbf{S}^{M})
-bimodule which vanishes in odd degrees and is graded-free as one-sided
\mathbf{S}^{M}
-module. Moreover, if
\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{I}
is a sub-ideal, then the inclusion
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{J}}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}
induces an injective map of graded
(\mathbf{S}^{M},\mathbf{S}^{M})
-bimodules

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{J}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}.

Démonstration.

We may assume that
\mathcal{I}
is finite because there is only a finite number of
[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
such that
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}\neq\emptyset
. The first statement is proven by induction on
\#\mathcal{I}
and
subsection 7.4
. The second statement is a direct consequence of the first one.
∎

Consequently, we may view
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{J}}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
as subspace of
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
by taking
\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
.

Proposition 7.9
.

For any pair of ideals
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
, let
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
be the product ideal introduced in
subsection 6.1
. Then the convolution product

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{J}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}

factorises through the subspace
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}}\subset\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}
.

Démonstration.

It follows from
subsection 6.7
that the projection

\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}\times_{\mathcal{P}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{X}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{J}}\to\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}

lies in
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}}
. It follows that the image of

\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{J}}\to\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}

lies in
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}}
.
∎

7.10
Convolution product over good strata

For each
y\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
, we have two ideals
\mathcal{I}_{y}=\left\{[w]\;;\;w\leq y\right\}
and
\mathcal{J}_{y}=\mathcal{I}_{y}\setminus[y]
of
\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
. Denote

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\leq y}=\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}_{y}},\quad\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{<y}=\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{J}_{y}}.

There is a short exact sequence of
(\mathbf{S}^{M},\mathbf{S}^{M})
-bimodules

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{<y}\to\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\leq y}\to\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{y}\to 0.

If
w,w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
are such that
\ell_{\xi}(ww^{\prime})=\ell_{\xi}(w)+\ell_{\xi}(w^{\prime})
, then
\ell(ww^{\prime})=\ell(w)+\ell(w^{\prime})
by
subsection 4.17
(iii)
and therefore there are inclusions

\mathcal{I}_{w}\cdot\mathcal{I}_{w^{\prime}}\subset\mathcal{I}_{ww^{\prime}},\quad\mathcal{I}_{w}\cdot\mathcal{J}_{w^{\prime}}\subset\mathcal{J}_{ww^{\prime}},\quad\mathcal{J}_{w}\cdot\mathcal{I}_{w^{\prime}}\subset\mathcal{J}_{ww^{\prime}}.

Thus
subsection 7.7
yields a map

(7.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{w^{\prime}}\cong(\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\leq w}/\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{<w})\otimes(\mathcal{H}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\leq w^{\prime}}/\mathcal{H}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{<w^{\prime}})\to\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\leq ww^{\prime}}/\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{<ww^{\prime}}=\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{ww^{\prime}}.

Proposition 7.12
.

Given
\nu\in\Xi
,
w,w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
such that
\ell_{\xi}(ww^{\prime})=\ell_{\xi}(w)+\ell_{\xi}(w^{\prime})
, let
\nu^{\prime}=w^{-1}\nu
and
\nu^{\prime\prime}=w^{\prime-1}\nu^{\prime}
.

(i)

The map

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{w^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{ww^{\prime}}

from (
) is surjective.

(ii)

There is an isomorphism of algebras
\mathbf{S}^{M}\cong\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu}_{e}
which is at the same time an
(\mathbf{S}^{M},\mathbf{S}^{M})
-bimodule isomorphism.

Remark 7.13
.

More generally, one can show that the associated graded algebra of the Bruhat filtration on the sum
\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}},\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
is isomorphic to a certain skew tensor product of the nil-Hecke algebra of
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}
with the “polynomial part”
\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}}}\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu}_{e}
. However, we will not need it.

Démonstration.

We proceed in steps.

Step 1
.

We show that the projection
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}
induces an isomorphism

\overline{\mu}:\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{w^{\prime}}\cong\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{ww^{\prime}}.

Let
\kappa\in\mathfrak{A}
be an alcove such that
\partial_{I_{\xi}}\kappa=\nu
. We view
w
and
w^{\prime}
as elements of
\mathcal{W}
via the splitting of shortest representative
\mathcal{W}_{\xi}\hookrightarrow N_{\mathcal{W}}(\mathcal{W}_{I})
of
subsection 4.17
(ii)
. From
subsection 4.5
, we deduce the following formula

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\dim(\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{n}/\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{n}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{w^{-1}\nu}_{n})=\#\left(w\Delta^{\kappa}\cap-\Delta^{\kappa}\right)=\ell(w),

from which we get inequalities

\displaystyle\ell(ww^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\dim(\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{n}/\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{n}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{n})\leq\sum_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\dim(\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{n}/\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{n}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{n}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\dim(\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{n}/\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{n}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{n})+\sum_{n\in\mathbf{Z}}\dim(\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{n}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{n}/\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{n}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{n}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{n})\leq\ell(w)+\ell(w^{\prime}).

By
subsection 4.17
(iii)
, we have
\ell(ww^{\prime})=\ell(w)+\ell(w^{\prime})
, from which we deduce
P^{\nu}_{0}\cap P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}\cap P^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{0}=P^{\nu}_{0}\cap P^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{0}
as well as
\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\eta}=\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\eta}
. By
subsection 6.17
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{w^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\cong G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\times^{P^{\nu}_{0}\cap P^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}\cap P^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{0}}\left(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime}}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\eta}\right)

\displaystyle\cong G_{\underline{\smash{0}}}\times^{P^{\nu}_{0}\cap P^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{0}}\left(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{u}^{\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\eta}\right)\cong\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{ww^{\prime}},

which proves the claim.

Step 2
.

We show that the map (
) agrees with the
Gysin map
of the closed embedding

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{ww^{\prime}}\cong\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{w^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{w}\times\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{w^{\prime}}.

It is a consequence of the transversality proven in Step 1
††
††

††

It is a cohomological analogue of the well-known property in the intersection theory that “refined Gysin map = Gysin map” in the case of transversal intersection. Indeed, the convolution product that we have defined makes use of the refined Gysin map from the diagonal embedding of
\mathcal{T}^{\nu^{\prime}}
, which is a regular embedding, see
[
4
, 6.3.2]
, see also the proof of
[
2
, 7.6.12]
. Consider the Cartesian square

\displaystyle\hbox to387.61pt{\vbox to58.01pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 193.80237pt\lower-29.11256pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

7.17
Parabolic subalgebras

In
subsection 5.6
, we have introduced an algebra

\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}=\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{\nu}},\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}\in\underline{\smash{\Xi}}^{\sigma}}\Ext^{*}_{L_{0,\diamond,q}^{\sigma}}(\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu}\mathscr{C})_{0}

for each
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-facet
\sigma
. This algebra can be thought of as parabolic subalgebra of
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
associated to the facet
\sigma
. The analysis of
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
in
section 6
and in
section 7
can equally be done for
\sigma
. In
subsection 7.17
, we will use the geometry of Steinberg varieties to study the map
\psi_{J}
appearing in
subsection 5.8
.

Let
\sigma\in\mathfrak{F}(\mathbb{E}^{M})
be an
\mathbb{E}^{M}
-facet and let
J\subset\Delta
denote its type (
subsection 4.4
) so that
\sigma\in\mathfrak{F}_{J}
. By
subsection 4.13

(iv)
, we have
I=I_{\xi}\subset J
.
For
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi^{\sigma}=\left\{\nu\in\Xi\;;\;\sigma\leq\nu\right\}
, put

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=(L^{\sigma}_{0}/P^{\nu\leq\sigma}_{0})\times(L^{\sigma}_{0}/P^{\nu^{\prime}\leq\sigma}_{0}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\sigma}^{\nu}

\displaystyle=L^{\sigma}_{0}\times^{P^{\sigma\leq\nu}_{0}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\sigma\leq\nu}_{\eta})\to\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{\eta},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\sigma}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}_{\sigma}^{\nu}\times_{\mathfrak{l}^{\sigma}_{\eta}}\mathcal{T}_{\sigma}^{\nu^{\prime}}.

There are
L^{\sigma}_{0}
-stable stratifications

\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma}=\bigcup_{[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}_{J}/\mathcal{W}_{I}}\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,w},\quad\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma}=\bigcup_{[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}_{J}/\mathcal{W}_{I}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,w}

so that
(P^{\sigma\leq\nu}_{0},P^{\sigma\leq\nu^{\prime}}_{0})\in\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,w}
for
w\in N_{\mathcal{W}_{J}}(\mathcal{W}_{I})
such that
w\nu^{\prime}=\nu
.

Proposition 7.18
.

The statements below hold for
\nu,\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}\in\Xi^{\sigma}
and for ideals
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}_{J}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
.

(i)

The subvarieties
\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{I}}\subset\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{I}}\subset\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma}
are closed.

(ii)

We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{I}}\times_{L^{\sigma}_{0}/P^{\sigma\leq\nu^{\prime}}_{0}}\mathcal{X}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{J}}\to\mathcal{X}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}},\quad\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{I}}\times_{L^{\sigma}_{0}/P^{\sigma\leq\nu^{\prime}}_{0}}\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{J}}\to\mathcal{Z}^{\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{I}\mathcal{J}}.

(iii)

For each
w\in\mathcal{W}_{\xi,J}
such that
w\underline{\smash{\nu}}^{\prime}=\underline{\smash{\nu}}
, there are isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,w}\cong L^{\sigma}_{0}/P^{\sigma\leq\nu}_{0}\cap P^{\sigma\leq w^{-1}\nu}_{0},\quad\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,w}\cong L^{\sigma}_{0}\times^{P^{\sigma\leq\nu}_{0}\cap P^{\sigma\leq w^{-1}\nu}_{0}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\sigma\leq\nu}_{\eta}\cap\mathfrak{u}^{\sigma\leq w^{-1}\nu}_{\eta}).

Démonstration.

See
subsection 6.7
and
subsection 6.17
.
∎

For
\nu\in\Xi^{\sigma}
, the inverse image of
\mathscr{C}_{\eta}
under the stack morphism
f:[\mathcal{T}^{\nu}_{\sigma}/L_{0,\diamond,q}^{\sigma}]\to[\mathrm{O}_{\eta}/{M_{0,\diamond,q}}]
is denoted by
\dot{\mathscr{C}}
. We have similarly a complex
\mathscr{K}_{\sigma}
on
\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma}
such that

\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma}:=\Ext^{*}_{L_{0,\diamond,q}^{\sigma}}(\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu}\mathscr{C})\cong\mathrm{H}^{*}_{L_{0,\diamond,q}^{\sigma}}(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma},\mathscr{K}_{\sigma}).

As in
subsection 7.7
, we put a filtration on
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma}
by defining for each ideal
\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}_{J}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
the following space

\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{I}}=\mathrm{H}^{*}_{L_{0,\diamond,q}^{\sigma}}(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{I}},i^{!}_{\mathcal{I}}\mathscr{K}_{\sigma}),\quad i_{\mathcal{I}}:\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{I}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma}.

For each
[w]\in\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}_{J}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
, we set

\mathrm{H}^{*}_{L_{0,\diamond,q}^{\sigma}}(\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,w},i^{!}_{w}\mathscr{K}_{\sigma}),\quad i_{w}:\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,w}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma}.

Then
subsection 7.4
and
subsection 7.10
also have analogues for
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,w}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{I}}
.

Now we consider the spiral induction
\Ind_{\sigma}
, the functoriality of which yields a map
\psi_{\sigma}
for
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\Xi^{\sigma}
:

(7.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma}=\Ext^{*}_{L_{0,\diamond,q}^{\sigma}}(\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C},\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C})\xrightarrow{\psi_{\sigma}}\Ext^{*}_{{G_{\underline{\smash{0}},\diamond,q}}}(\Ind_{\sigma}\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C},\Ind_{\sigma}\Ind^{\sigma}_{\nu^{\prime}}\mathscr{C})\cong\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}.

Proposition 7.20
.

The map
\psi_{\sigma}
is injective and for each ideal
\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{W}_{I}\backslash\mathcal{W}_{J}/\mathcal{W}_{I}
, we have

\psi_{\sigma}(\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma,\mathcal{I}})=\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{I}}.

In particular,
\psi_{\sigma}
induces an isomorphism
\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\sigma}\cong\mathcal{H}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}_{\leq w^{J}_{0}}
, where
w^{J}_{0}
is the longest element of the finite Coxeter subgroup
(\mathcal{W}_{J},J)\subset(\mathcal{W},\Delta)
.

Démonstration.

Set

\displaystyle\pi^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\sigma}^{\nu}

\displaystyle=L^{\sigma}_{0}\times^{P^{\sigma\leq\nu}_{0}}(\mathrm{O}_{\eta}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{\nu}_{\eta})\to\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{\eta},

\displaystyle\pi^{*}\mathcal{Z}_{\sigma}^{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}_{\sigma}^{\nu}\times_{\mathfrak{p}^{\sigma}_{\eta}}\mathcal{T}_{\sigma}^{\nu^{\prime}}.

We have the following two Cartesian squares
